# Architecture map

Where the code for each part of taxjson lives, so a diagnosis or a change can
go straight to the right file. taxjson is a Python package: a Canadian
capital-gains toolkit (ACB, superficial loss, income, filing forms) built from
broker CSV exports, with an experimental US engine. The command is `tjs` (`taxjson` is the same program).

`src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_run.py` is the entry point: `main`, the two names
`taxjson_missing_history` imports from it, and the two a published
`taxjson-fetch` imports (taxjson.lib imports nothing from bin). Tests import from where a name lives. The commands are in `src/taxjson/cli/`, one module per
group of the help page (Before you trade's in two, `plan.py` and `tips.py`; `parser.py` builds the argument parser, `main.py` dispatches,
`common.py` holds what several groups read). The run is in `src/taxjson/pipeline/`:
`run.py`, `stages.py` and `sanity.py` over `inputs.py`, `freshness.py`, `diagnostics.py`,
`config.py`, `tools.py` and `messages.py`; a module there never imports a command, and
`run_state.py` holds what the commands rebind, and `RunState`: what one run's stages leave for each other and what a command has warned about once, read as `run_state.current`; a nested command gets a copy (`nested_command`), kept only if its caller shows its stderr (`relayed_nested`). `lib`'s once-only warnings are in sets from `src/taxjson/lib/once.py` — `said_set`, `own_process`, `forget_all`: a stage whose stderr is its .diag (pipeline/dispatch's run_cmd given a stdout file) runs as a process of its own, and a tjs command run from the top (cli/main's main, not nested) starts afresh.

Several files are very large. The gains engines left lib/core.py
for `src/taxjson/engine/` in step 10 (they were about 14.1k lines; the largest
module now is `src/taxjson/engine/ca/walk.py` (about 1.6k)); the shim that
re-exported their names from lib/core.py went after v1.0.0: import each name
from its module there. `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/ib_extractor.py` (about
8.9k), `src/taxjson/lib/corp_actions.py`, `src/taxjson/lib/missing_history.py`,
`src/taxjson/lib/tax_logic.py` and `src/taxjson/lib/checklist.py` (about 4k to
6k each) and `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/rbc_direct.py` (about 3k) follow. Do not read them top to bottom: search them for the function name
listed below ("def name("), then read around the hit.

Conventions used here:

- `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `cmd_run`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_account`: a command `foo-bar` is handled by its `cmd_<name>` function in the module of its help-page group (dashes as underscores), with a few older names (`events` is `src/taxjson/cli/listings.py` — `cmd_transactions`, `trades` is `cmd_buysell`, `list` is `src/taxjson/cli/positions.py` — `cmd_positions`, `sum` is `src/taxjson/cli/estimate.py` — `cmd_summary`); its parser is in the same module: `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `register`, `set_defaults`; `src/taxjson/cli/parser.py` — `_build_parser` calls each group's.
- Each stand-alone tool `taxjson-<tool>` is `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_<tool>.py` with a `main()`; `tjs run` calls them through `_cmd("taxjson-<tool>")`, usually in the same process. `src/taxjson/cli/main.py` — `_subparser`: an unknown flag after a command is refused with that command's usage line (`main` parses with `parse_known_args`).
- The logic is in `src/taxjson/lib/`; tools are in `src/taxjson/bin/`, commands in `src/taxjson/cli/`. A view usually reads the files `tjs run` left in the project's `work/` and `reports/` folders.
- Rule ids such as CA-SL-02 or US-WASH-22 are the statements `tjs tax-logic` prints (`src/taxjson/lib/tax_logic.py`); each is a constant in `src/taxjson/rules/ids.py` (`CA_SL_02`). Code names a rule by its constant or cites its id in a comment, and tests cite them. Search the constant (`CA_SL_02`) or the id to find the code that implements a rule; `scripts/check_tax_rules.py --where CA-SL-02` lists the tests that pin it.
- Tests live in `tests/`, mostly one file per fix round or feature; search a function name there to find what pins it.
- The playbook of known problems is `docs/troubleshooting.md`; the house style for everything printed is `docs/output-style.md`.
- On each bullet, the names after the path are literal strings in that file: functions, classes, constants, or a distinctive message or shell word.

## The CLI and command dispatch

`main` builds one argparse parser with a sub-parser per command and runs the
chosen handler. The help page groups commands under `_COMMAND_GROUPS`
headings. Before a handler runs, `_main` applies the project guards: `-C DIR`,
unknown or path-shaped account names, and the country check (a command the
other country owns is refused). Several commands on one line (`tjs run sum`)
are split into segments and run in order.

- `src/taxjson/cli/main.py` — `main`, `_main`; `src/taxjson/cli/parser.py` — `_build_parser`, `_GROUPS` (each group module's `register` adds its commands' parsers, beside the handlers; a shared flag or help text is in `src/taxjson/cli/common.py` — `_PERIOD_HELP`, `_add_instrument_filters`, `_add_deduction_flags`, `_add_staking_parser`), `_COMMAND_GROUPS`, `_GroupedHelpParser`, `_dev_checkout_present`, `_MAINTAINER_GROUP`; `src/taxjson/cli/main.py` — `_enforce_command_country`, `_refuse_unknown_account`, `_split_command_segments`, `_command_index`, `_chain_accounts`, `_prefix_dir` (the folder the global options name, in any spelling argparse takes), `_help_country`, `_failure_headline`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/messages.py` — `_die`, `_current_cmd` (the command a message names: the one dispatched, or an in-process `cmd_*` call's own): the entry point, the parser and the grouped help page (the other country's commands hidden, and the Maintainer group outside a development checkout); the checks applied before every command; command chaining (`tjs run sum`; `--` separates; an account, folder or symbol named like a command stays the command's argument); how a failed stage or bad input becomes one short message.
- `src/taxjson/lib/cli_diag.py` — `guard_main`, `run_top_level`, `labelled_usage_errors`, `describe_input_error`, `tolerant_stdout`, `_closed_streams_to_devnull`, `_RoomGuard`, `_guarded_streams`: the top-level wrapper every tool runs under (broken pipes, unreadable input, usage errors, the settling streams that print a multi-line message's blank line; a standard stream closed at the start is /dev/null, and one a full device refuses goes there, to the process's end (a `SystemExit` text, an atexit note, the interpreter's flush), stdout's loss one `cannot write the output` line once the command has run (exit 2, or the command's own exit when not 0), stderr's said nowhere: TJ-4831; an engine stopped at a money site still mixing float in is one `error:` line naming the site, exit 1: `src/taxjson/model/money.py` — `MoneySiteError`, `stopped_text`).
- `src/taxjson/lib/country.py` — `COMMAND_COUNTRY`, `command_country_problem`, `flag_country_problems`, `given_flags`: which commands and flags belong to one country.

## Stand-alone tools and in-process dispatch

Each pipeline stage is also a console script (`[project.scripts]` in
pyproject.toml). The scripts go through a trampoline that sets an owner-only
umask. `tjs run` builds the same command lines, but `run_cmd` runs a
known tool in this process unless a TTY is needed or `TAXJSON_DISPATCH=subprocess`.

- `pyproject.toml` — `project.scripts`, `taxjson-merge2`, `taxjson-gains`, `taxjson-corp-actions`: the list of console scripts and the module each one runs.
- `src/taxjson/bin/_entry.py` — `private_umask`, `__getattr__`, `console_prog` (a bin module's console-script name; `_PROG_ALIASES` spells out four, two of which differ from the `taxjson-<x>` rule; here, not in lib: lib names no bin module): the trampoline from `taxjson-<tool>` to the module's `main()`; `src/taxjson/bin/__init__.py` — `_os.umask` sets the same umask for `python -m taxjson.bin.<tool>` (while `sys.argv[0]` is `-m`).
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/dispatch.py` — `run_cmd`, `tool_module`, `_use_subprocess`, `TAXJSON_DISPATCH`: in-process execution of a tool, with the subprocess fallback (the pipeline's: it imports a bin tool by name, which lib may not); `src/taxjson/lib/dispatch.py` — `python_module_argv`: every child Python process is launched through `python_module_argv` (`-P`) so the current directory is never on `sys.path`; the tool commands (`src/taxjson/pipeline/tools.py` — `_cmd`; `src/taxjson/cli/common.py` — `_self_cmd`) are its forms, which `src/taxjson/pipeline/dispatch.py` — `tool_module` runs in process (`tests/features/checks/test_sanity_usage_child_pythons_and_relayed_errors.py` — `python_launches`: no other launch in the package).
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/tools.py` — `_cmd`, `run_to_file`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/diagnostics.py` — `run_capture`; `src/taxjson/cli/common.py` — `_exec_tool`; `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `_run_cmd`: how the orchestrator calls a tool and captures its stdout and `.diag` stderr.

## The tjs run orchestrator

`cmd_run` reads and checks taxjson.toml, takes the project lock, builds the FX
rate file, then runs every account's stages. It parses all accounts first
(crypto sends, own-account moves, cross-listings and Questrade codes need
every account's evidence), then builds each account's books and gains, then
the cross-account wash passes, the cross-account reports, the filed-year
drift check and the end-of-run summary. Every run rebuilds every stage (no
switch skips one); the records it writes are read by the sweep and the views.

- `src/taxjson/pipeline/freshness.py` — `needs_rebuild` (by content: a record per output under `.deps/`, a report's under work/.deps/reports/; a declared input gone or no longer declared is a change by itself, no marker: TJ-1807), `commit_records` (a run vouches for what it built when it completes, keeping another module's fields: `_OWN`), `record_now`, `package_fingerprint`, `FolderInputError` (a folder declared as an input: refused; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stage_table.py` — `_refuse_folder`, the one line every command gives for it); the rebuild rule of the fetched rates alone (a run builds every stage, and a view its full-history gains, every time: `force`; TJ-4307). The records' format, place and hasher, one for the run and the views: `src/taxjson/lib/records.py` — `record_path`, `read_record`, `write_record`, `set_record_field`, `sha256_file`, `RECORD_FORMAT` (an older taxjson's record reads as none: the first run after the upgrade rebuilds everything, TJ-4266).
- The stamps a stage declares for the lines it reads from files it does not name whole, each rewritten only when it changes: `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_missing_history_deps`, `MISSING_HISTORY_STATE` (the OPENING cost=unknown lines a gains run reads: `src/taxjson/lib/missing_history.py` — `missing_history_reads`, `missing_history_maps`, `_declaration_map`), `_ib_tt_state` (the .tt lines the IB parse reads: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/ib_extractor.py` — `tt_reads_text`), `_write_source_stamps` (an account's sources list and inputs fingerprint, again after a crypto account's sends stage); `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_merge_sheltered` (the sheltered books' merge, called from `_run_sheltered`, gated: the stage table's sheltered row, `_RUN_STAGES`, `_sheltered_build`).
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/stage_table.py` — `ROWS`, `TABLE`, `Stage`, `StageCtx`, `Inst`, `File`, `Out`, `Tree`, `STAMPS`, `TREES`, `SOURCES`, `GAINS_READS`, `BOOK`, `resolve`, `member_books`, `instances`, `output`, `execute`, `bound`, `Steps`, `Ready`, `REACHED_LOG_ENV`: the stage table (PLAN step 12), a row per output a freshness ask decides: what it is built per (the project, an account, a broker group, a .tt file, a blend), its output, its inputs as typed references (a `File` declared present or absent, an `Out` of another row or of a stamp for each instance it reads, a `Tree` of files the run selects), when it runs, its own grounds and the rows it runs `after` (the native books, after the raw row's finding). The executor is the package's one `needs_rebuild` ask: for each instance, the stamps first (`prepare`), the ask over the row's inputs, the build (every time; when stale or on a ground only for a `fetched` row `reuse`d: the rates, `_rates_grounds`; `reuse` of any other row is refused, TJ-4307), the every-run echoes and --strict gates; a merge builds from the books its row declares, in the members' order (`member_books`); a build vouched for at once when asked (`at_once`: the view commands' on-demand builds); a declared folder one line, exit 2, for every command (`_refuse_folder`); each output it ran kept by output in `src/taxjson/pipeline/run_state.py` — `stage_outputs` (two rows share one: base and crypto_base, gains_all and gains_all_split, split and wash, holdings and crypto_holdings). The renders and reports are rows too (TJ-1828, TJ-4260), their step lines said every run, in `src/taxjson/pipeline/stage_table.py` — `ROWS`: an account's validation, holdings handoff (built again after the passes across accounts, its wash file declared: `MOVED_COST`), summary and wash twin with the diagnostics their banners fold (`_sum_diags`, `_wash_sum_diags`), its report model once the passes ran (`washed`), the reports across accounts (`RADAR_READS`, `radar_json`, the day the radar counts from in `STAMPS`' today) and the fees report (`_fees_books`). What a run's row instances built is listed in work/.deps/run.json (`commit_run`, `last_run`: each instance by what names it, `_ID`); a full run sweeps what the last one's instances built and its own did not, before the reports across accounts read the books (`sweep_vanished`, `LATE_ROWS`, `swept_accounts`: a removed account is not warned about), each file the table's (`derived` from the listed instance, an export group's `_GROUPS`, never an elections file `_NEVER_SWEPT`; the files beside it the row's `companions`, a removed account's own files `account_files` with `VIEW_ROWS`), never one through a link (`_sweepable`), and only one its record vouches for (`_vouched`: what it kept and why, `Swept`; TJ-4269, TJ-4264, TJ-4273, TJ-4274; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_sweep`), an account's run removing the parse of an export group gone from its inputs only where taxjson can vouch for it (with records in work/, `_has_records`, what a record vouches for and the `_transfers.json` beside a parse that goes; with none, a file named as an export group's parse), the rest moved to work/kept/ with its `.diag` (`KEPT_DIR`, `move_aside`: a link as the link, still leading where it led; `OTHER_BYTES`, `NO_RECORD`), where no reader of the account's files sees it, and named once, a folder left and named (`LEFT`); a work/ that is a link to a folder in the project is that folder, swept as work/ (`_linked_work`, in `_sweepable`) (`src/taxjson/pipeline/stage_table.py` — `stale_parse_fates`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_sweep_stale_parses`: TJ-5071), and an older taxjson's files beside the records go with its first run (`_LEGACY`); a map removed is said from a report's record that names it (`recorded_input`). Each module binds its rows' steps: `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_STAGES`, `_AccountRun`, `_rates_build`, `_parse_prepare`, `_parse_build`, `_corp_build`, `_tt_build`, `_crypto_filled_prepare`, `_base_build`, `_gains_prepare`, `_raw_every_run`, `_native_gains_build`, `_BlendRun`, `_blend_base_build`, `_blend_gains_prepare`, `_blend_gains_build`, `_split_build`, `_split_every_run`, `_wash_prepare`, `_wash_build`, `_wash_every_run`, `_crypto_validate_build`, `_holdings_build`, `stage_moved_holdings`, `_sum_build`, `_wash_sum_build`, `_report_build`, `stage_reports`, `_CrossRun`, `_radar_prepare`, `_write_today`, `_Fees`, `_fees_prepare`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_RUN_STAGES` (the sheltered merge); `src/taxjson/cli/common.py` — `_ON_DEMAND`, `_gains_all_build`, `_gains_all_blend_build`, `_gains_all_split_build` (the full-history gains `_gains_all_file` and `_gains_all_blend` build for a view, every time and once per command: `_built_this_command`, `_built_now`, `src/taxjson/pipeline/run_state.py` — `views_built`, `begin_command`). Held by `tests/test_stage_table.py` — `TestTheResolver`, `TestTheExecutor`, `test_a_derived_row_is_never_reused`, `test_every_ask_is_the_executor_s`, `test_only_a_fetched_row_reuses_its_record`, `test_every_row_is_bound_once`, `test_the_rows_that_share_an_output`; every row reached on the golden books: `tests/test_freshness_edges.py` — `test_every_row_of_the_stage_table_is_reached`.
- `tests/test_freshness_edges.py` — `builds`, `judge`, `ALLOWED`, `STAMPED`, `VARIANTS`, `BUILD_ON_DEMAND`, `call_sites`, `test_a_view_run_again_builds_again`: the freshness-edge audit (TJ-2324): every project golden book, and a variant with a missing-history line, run with every file open traced (`tests/_opentrace/sitecustomize.py` — `_hook`, `_Finder`, `_frames`, `_API`), each read a build makes (the innermost build's) held to its `needs_rebuild` declaration (an undeclared one fails, unless `ALLOWED` gives its reason or `STAMPED` a stamp the build declares), and every call site reached; reads between builds are not judged (TJ-2498). Every file of the project the run opens is one the input fingerprint the filing commands' staleness refusal compares hashes (`src/taxjson/lib/checklist.py` — `input_fingerprint`, `_input_paths`), or `NOT_BOOK_INPUTS` says why the books never read it (`test_every_project_file_the_run_reads_is_fingerprinted`). Then the on-demand commands again: each builds every full-history file it reads again, once (no record's word: TJ-4307; the no-change `run --fast` test went with the flag); the run's own bookkeeping is written only when it changed (`src/taxjson/lib/safe_write.py` — `write_changed`; `src/taxjson/lib/compare.py` — `_holds`).
- `tests/test_record_edges.py` — `CASES`, `_case`, `COMPARED`, `_built_on_demand`, `TestTheLocksAreDeclared`, `TestAFolderWhereAFileWasExpected`: after one edit (an OPENING cost=unknown line, an IB ticker change's .tt line, a map line for an opening's symbol, a [[distributions]] entry's opening, an input of each blend row), `tjs stats --all-history` builds what a rebuild of its files from scratch does, and the run's records declare what each stage read; a US project's filed-year lock is on the gains rows' records; a folder where a file was expected runs as before the guard, or is one line, from `tjs run` and a view command alike. `tests/features/run/test_every_run_rebuilds_everything.py` — `TestEveryRunRebuildsEverything`: a run with nothing changed builds every stage again, and `--fast` is an unknown option, nothing written.
- `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `cmd_run`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_acquire_run_lock` (held through `safe_write.file_lock`); `src/taxjson/pipeline/config.py` — `_refuse_outside_dir_links` (a written folder linked outside the project stops every command: `src/taxjson/cli/main.py` — `_refuse_outside_links`, the project-level check `_main` runs before any command whatever reads its config, `_LINK_CHECK_FREE_CMDS` the commands it leaves alone; `src/taxjson/pipeline/config.py` — `load_config`); `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_loose_project_dirs` (the once-per-run warning about a folder other users can read); `src/taxjson/pipeline/freshness.py` — `needs_rebuild`, `_package_fingerprint`, `_package_files`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_account`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/diagnostics.py` — `write_holdings_toml` (each account's holdings report, a crypto account's from its own books); `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_wash_pass`, `stage_blended_wash_pass`, `stage_cross_reports`, `stage_fees`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/diagnostics.py` — `collect_diagnostics` (a blend mirror's message about another account's file tagged `[<account>]`: `_diag_block_account`), `echo_attention_lines`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_first_run_summary`: the run, its one-run-per-project lock and its records; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_account` is the per-account chain (parse, corp actions, .tt files, transfer arrivals, merge, gains, raw holdings, the `.sum` report); then the passes across accounts; what each stage printed, folded into the console and the `.sum` DIAGNOSTICS section; the closing summary.
- `src/taxjson/lib/first_run.py` — `collect`, `render`, `render_blocks`, `uncovered_short_sales`, `unknown_cost_sales`, `_opening_where`, `unknown_cost_phrase`, `unknown_cost_lines`, `unknown_cost_advice`, `line_name`, `unknown_cost_row`, `_unknown_cost_item`, `_unknown_cost_detail`, `logged_openings`, `engine_booking`, `zero_cost_positions`, `income_without_position`, `in_kind_not_booked`, `SUMMARY_FILE`: the "what to check next" counts at the end of a run (`unknown_cost_sales`: the year's rows routed to manual reporting, each with the OPENING cost=unknown rows the engine wrote on the record and their lines, named with the cost to add; `unknown_cost_advice` the one rule the run's texts and the checklist name a line by; `_unknown_cost_item` the always-one-line closing item; backlog TJ-2770, TJ-3208 to TJ-3216) (`in_kind_not_booked`: the in-kind moves with a registered account NOT booked, from work/in_kind.json); `engine_booking` reads the gains files so a sale is called "NOT in `tjs sum`" only when they lack it (the run's mid-run note, before the wash pass, reads the plain ones: `prefer_wash=False`). `parser_warnings`, `_parser_items`: the parsers' warnings marked for the list, each with its account (`src/taxjson/lib/out.py` — `END_OF_RUN_MARK`, `shown_topic`, `unmarked`: the console and the .sum drop the mark, `src/taxjson/pipeline/tools.py` — `_echo_captured`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/diagnostics.py` — `collect_diagnostics`; the bookings the owner ruled are said, not refused: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/generic.py` — `_say_zero_prices`; `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/ib_extractor.py` — `Proceeds is 0; booked at the`), read back from work/*.json.diag and kept in reports/run_summary.json only when there is one.
- `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `cmd_run`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_cmd_run` (its parts: `_run_config`, `_run_folders`, `_run_maps`, `_run_duplicate_inputs`, `_run_first_pass`, `_run_sheltered`, `_run_taxable`, `_run_reports`, `_run_pending_elections`, `_run_cross_reports`, `_run_finish`; it compares no positions with the holdings files: `tjs sanity` is its own step); `src/taxjson/pipeline/messages.py` — `_run_concise`, `_say`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/tools.py` — `_echo_captured`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/run_state.py` — `RunState`, `begin_run`, `details_hidden`, `echo_last_error`: `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `cmd_run` turns the one-line console on for the run and its stages (`lib/stage_msg.CONCISE_ENV`, without `--details` on a person's console) and calls `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_cmd_run`, the run itself; `src/taxjson/pipeline/messages.py` — `_say` prints a warning or note as one line (its `short` form, or the headline and the command its detail names), an error with its fix lines; `details_hidden` makes the closing line name `tjs run --details`. `src/taxjson/lib/first_run.py` — `concise_items`, `CONCISE_BUDGET`: the closing block under "Before you trust these numbers", one line per finding, at most six lines.

## Input discovery and broker detection

Each account's folder under `inputs/` is scanned; every CSV is routed to a
parser by its content (header signature), then by its name prefix, never by
guessing. A file that matches two parsers or none stops the run with a
message naming the file.

- `src/taxjson/pipeline/config.py` — `input_files`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/inputs.py` — `group_inputs_detailed`, `detect_broker`, `_report_detection`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/config.py` — `spreadsheet_inputs`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_duplicate_input_files`, `_sweep_retired_exports`, `_warn_shared_broker_accounts`, `_source_brokerages`: the folder scan, the one line per file saying how it was read, and checks on duplicate, retired or shared exports.
- `src/taxjson/parsers/settings.py` — `ParseSettings`: what a parse is told about the project, built once by `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_brokerage.py` — `_parse_settings` (from the tool's flags, every choice an input: the country only canonicalised, for the law a message cites; the parse's basis, the stablecoin model and the plans taxed as ordinary, from `src/taxjson/rules/country.py` — `parse_basis`, `stablecoin_model`, `NEUTRAL_BASIS` through `ParseSettings.for_country`, refused beside a country when left out; a foreign issuer's return of capital is the characterize step's, below) and applied by `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `configure` (the fields and the account context; `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/ib_extractor.py` — `configure` adds IB's deferred messages); the tool's attribute pokes and probes gone (step 5h); the stablecoin note's gate the registry's `crypto` flag. The basis fields a parser's rows depend on, its basis inputs: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `BASIS_INPUTS` (none on the base). Kraken and Coinbase name the stablecoin model there: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/kraken.py` — `BASIS_INPUTS`; `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/coinbase.py` — `BASIS_INPUTS`. The plans taxed as ordinary decide the taxable-versus-registered verdict alone, a message or a refusal and never a row: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `check_account_plans`, `plan_shelter_of`; `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/questrade.py` — `_qt_taxable_hint`. The conformance check's identical rows hold the declarations (below).
- `src/taxjson/parsers/registry.py` — `ParserSpec`, `SPECS` (every parser: id, display name, spellings, module and class, tier, whether it extracts corporate actions, whether it reads an exchange's coins, name prefixes and words, whether it reads the run's rates: `reads_rates`, Coinbase and Kraken, the only parsers the parse stage gives them to and declares them for, whether its parse builds the export's cash events: `builds_cash`, IB, Questrade, RBC, Coinbase and Kraken), `TIERS`, `spec`, `parser_class` (of a spec or a spelling; a parser that cannot load is a ValueError naming it), `recorded_id` (the fold a recorded source brokerage takes), `corp_action_brokers`, `rate_reading_brokers` (the parse stage gives the rates file to them only and declares it, through pipeline/config.py's constant; TJ-2497, TJ-2508), `cash_event_brokers` (the FX-on-cash ledger's brokers, the parsers whose parse builds the cash events), `display_names`, `name_prefixes`, `name_words`: the one list the detection tables, the brokerage tool and the corporate-action brokers are generated from (the content detectors stay detect's own, held to the list by the test); `tests/test_parser_registry.py` holds them to it.
- `src/taxjson/parsers/example.py` — `SampleBrokerage`, `SampleRow`: the shape of a parser on the template, for the scaffolding tool's prompt and a contributor's first read (a made-up broker; not registered, `tjs` never runs it).
- `src/taxjson/parsers/protocol.py` — `Outcome`, `KINDS` (skip, nonevent, reported, consumed, build; another refused when built), `Row` (what a parser's row object has at least: its cells, its line, the builder classify chose through `build`), `KNOWN_NONEVENT_PREFIX`, `REPORTED_PREFIX`, `reported` (a skip's three label classes), `RowCounts` (what a parse counted, the tools' and the kit's one reading), `SURFACE` (the template's public members by role, frozen before the IB conversion; tests/test_parse_protocol.py holds the base to it), `ROW_KINDS`, `CASH_KINDS`, `row_kind` (the kinds of row a parse books and of the cash events an export holds, PLAN step 16: each a name the rows carry, an action, a row type, an expiry kept in `expiry_rows`; read off a row, never stored), `builds`, `parser_kinds` (the kinds each builder declares it books, and a parser class's union): the parse phase by phase; `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `parse_file` (the template: `read`, then per row `classify` and `build`, then `finish`, then the shared closing steps: the settlement clamp on `_expiries`, the split fills on the rows without an `id` of their own, the rows `note_cash` kept (`_cash_rows`) cleared, the parser's finish having built `cash_events` from them, `after_marks` (the parser's step after them), the skip summary, `release` whatever happened; `parse_rows` the parse up to the parser's finish, for a parser reading another file for hints (Webull's sibling exports, each read by a parser of its class with its settings); `recount` (the one move of the counts: a finish moving a consumed row's count to a skipped class, or a skipped row's to consumed; more than was counted so refused), `skip_classes` (the one reading of the skip labels' classes, for the summary, the tool and a parser's zero-transactions reason); `row_line_of` the line each row's messages name, the row's `line`); `tests/test_parse_protocol.py` pins it with a stub parser. The first parser on it: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/rbc_direct.py` — `read` (the account context, the counters, the reorganization pairing's pre-pass; what the later phases need as `_RbcParse`, released by `release`), `classify`, `build` (`_dispatch` and the stamping), `finish`, `release`.
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/detect.py` — `NAME_PREFIXES`, `NAME_WORDS` (the registry's views), `DETECTORS` (its own, one per registry parser but generic), `detect`, `mapping_choice` (the generic importer's choice, a dangling sidecar counted, two fitting layouts `AmbiguousBroker`; a signature's fit beside a supported export's match is ambiguous too), `detect_broker`, `Detection`, `AmbiguousBroker`, `content_matches`, `name_hint`, `looks_like_ib_text`, `same_broker_siblings`, `positions_only_text` (a positions-ONLY report: an RBC Holdings Export and its "as of" date), `kind_label`: content-first detection (each parser's header signature is the detector the registry names), then the file-name hint; IB statement sniffing and sibling files. A parser never imports the detector: its caller sets the finder of an export's siblings, `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `sibling_finder` (asked through `_same_broker_siblings`; None, a bare parse, the file alone). The callers that set it: `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_brokerage.py` — `main`; `src/taxjson/parsers/conformance.py` — `_fresh` (taxjson-redact's check reads each copy alone in a folder of its own, `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_redact.py` — `_parse_quiet`). The readers: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/kraken.py` — `_kraken_siblings`, `kraken_named` (the name test Kraken's cash reader shares); `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/webull.py` — `_sibling_exports`. A test sets it with `tests/_siblings.py` — `with_siblings`. The parsers' display names (the registry's view, which symbol_codes and the reports read too) are `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `DISPLAY_NAMES`.
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_detect_brokerage.py` — `detect_brokerage`, `cannot_detect_message`, `main`: the `taxjson-detect-brokerage` tool, the same decision for one file.
- `src/taxjson/bin/xlsx_to_csv.py` — `convert_xlsx_to_csv`, `read_sheet`, `pick_sheet`, `_cell_text`, `_clean_numeric_commas`: turns an .xlsx export into CSV before parsing (openpyxl, each cell as the sheet shows it, no header inference).

## The parse stage

`taxjson-brokerage` loads one registered parser, runs it over the account's
files and writes normalized transaction JSON. Every row is checked against
the schema. ticker.map EXTRACT lines (security overrides) are applied here,
and broker account numbers are hashed before they reach the output.

- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_brokerage.py` — `main`, `load_security_overrides`, `apply_security_override`, `stamp_source_accounts`, `final_record_cut`, `_dedup_evidence`, `_restated_id`, `_restated_evidence`, `_only_nonevents`, `_refusal`; `src/taxjson/lib/privacy.py` — `hash_broker_account`: the parse tool and the parser registry calls; trimming, evidence dedup, refusing a row (or custody row) two downloads restate, and refusing an export that yields nothing usable.
- `src/taxjson/parsers/registry.py` — `SPECS`, `parser_class`: the id-to-class registry the parse tool reads (it left `lib/core.py` in step 5e).
- `src/taxjson/characterize/__init__.py` — `characterize_rows`: the country's reading of what a parse found, applied by `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_brokerage.py` — `characterize_rows`, `_brokerage_parse_file` to each file's rows as it is parsed (the re-extraction's recommendation 8; a parse gives the same rows under every country). Its first slice, `src/taxjson/characterize/foreign_roc.py` — `foreign_roc_treatment` (over `src/taxjson/rules/country.py` — `foreign_roc_choice`, the rule on the two values, which `src/taxjson/lib/country.py` `foreign_roc_mode` wraps for a project's settings), `characterize_foreign_roc`, `S90_NOTE`: an IB return of capital from a non-Canadian issuer, which the parse leaves a dividend row marked `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/descriptions.py` — `ISSUER_ROC_KEY`, booked a dividend (CA-ACB-08) or the cost reduction a Canadian issuer's is (`src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `roc_adjust_row`; CA-ACB-09, US-ROC-01). The marked rows are split fills only of each other and never an ordinary dividend's beside (`src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `disambiguate_split_fills`; IB's accrual backfill; TJ-2594).
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_extractors.py` — `main`: lists the registered parser ids and classes.
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/inputs.py` — `_mapping_choice` and `_layout_files` (the parse's mapping and layout dependencies), `_parse_reads`, `_parse_outputs`, `_reused_parse`, `_split_generic_groups`: how `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_account` calls the parse per broker group and reuses a cached parse.

## Broker parsers: shared base and schema

Every parser subclasses `BaseBrokerage` and fills the parse template's phases, `read`, `classify`, `build` and `finish` (`parse_file` is the template's, `src/taxjson/parsers/protocol.py`; no parser overrides it since Interactive Brokers' staged conversion, step 5h (8)). The base
class holds the shared helpers: OCC option symbols, currency suffixes, text
reading (the strict number parsers are the toolbox's, `parsers/toolbox/numbers.py`),
settlement dates and skip accounting. The schema module is the
one table of what a normalized row must look like.

- `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/errors.py` — `BrokerageParseError`: the error a parser raises rather than guess (its home since step 5a; `lib/brokerages/base.py` re-exports it).
- `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/numbers.py` — `parse_strict_number`, `desc_number`, `check_comma_grouping` (the strict grammar for a required cell and a number in description text: thousands commas only, accounting parentheses, the unicode minus, a currency sign), `strict_money`, `_ctx` (the crypto exports' cell parser: blank is 0, one sign, `ValueError` naming the field), `MINUS_CHARS`; moved as they stood from `base.py` and `_crypto_common.py`, which re-export them (step 5a).
- `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/descriptions.py` — `PIL_DESC_RE`, `FILL_MARK_RE` (the split-fill mark base.py writes, its readers' one pattern), `ISSUER_ROC_KEY`, `REINV_CUR_RE`, `REINV_CUR`, `REINV_PRICE_RE`, `reinvest_identity_error`, `reinvest_row_price`, `_parse_div_qty_rate`, `_DIV_QTY_ON_SHS_RE`, `_DIV_PER_SHARE_RE`, `_DIV_CASH_DIVIDEND_RE`: what a description says, one grammar for RBC, Questrade and IB (step 5f); a dividend row's share count and per-share rate are among its facts (PLAN step 14 moved them from base.py, which still re-exports the function and the share-count pattern for the tests and lib/positions_check, since lib reads no toolbox). The option reader's strict wrapper is the base class's method, naming the file through a hook, and the underlying check its function: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `strict_option_from_description`, `_where_name`, `names_underlying`; the root-matching it reads is the engine's public `src/taxjson/lib/option_symbols.py` — `root_matches_stock`. The number grammar of description text: `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/numbers.py` — `DESC_NUMBER_RE`.
- `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/money.py` — `money_close` (a stated amount against the one its row implies: Questrade, IB, Kraken, Coinbase, generic and the reinvestment check, each with its own tolerances), `money_tolerance` (its band, also generic's commission fit's), `implied_commission_bounds` (the commission a row's money may imply: RBC Direct, Webull): the qty x price identity and its band, one copy (step 5; tests/test_one_copy.py); other identities (a total against its subtotal and fee, a fee against a fee, a quantity's) stay each parser's.
- `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/expiry.py` — `option_expiry_booking_date`, `non_option_expiry_booking_date`, `_DESC_EXPIRY_RE`, `clamp_settlement_across`: when an expiry happened. An option's expiry row Questrade or RBC Direct posts the next business day is booked on the contract's own expiry (at most a week before the posting), a right's or a warrant's on its description's AS OF / EXP date, and a trade in the expiring contract on its expiry day settles no later than the expiry (IB, Questrade, RBC Direct, Webull: per file through `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `clamp_settlement_to_expiry`, which keeps the rows on `expiry_rows`, and over an account's files in `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_brokerage.py` — `clamp_settlement_across`, `_brokerage_account_pass`). PLAN step 14 moved them from base.py, whose template keeps the three as its helpers (`src/taxjson/parsers/protocol.py` — `SURFACE`).
- `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/fee_coins.py` — `fee_coin_sale`, `_DUST_USD`, `_units`, `_usd_text`, `_TT_REMOVE`: the sale at FMV of a fee a crypto ledger took in a coin. A quantity under the books' zero worth at most `_DUST_USD` is left out with a note, one worth more refused. PLAN step 14 moved it from Kraken's parser, with the wording its refusals share with the parser. Its caller passes the basis, the fee's coin as the book names it and the currencies the book counts as cash: `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/kraken.py` — `_norm`, `_fiat` (a transfer's, a fiat funding's and a reward's fee: `_build_ledger_transfer`, `_build_fiat_funding`, `_income_row`).
- `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/staking.py` — `ledger_reward_legs`, `reward_value`, `valued_reward_legs`: a reward's books, the income at its fair value and the coins acquired at that value, the income alone for a reward the book counts as cash (a fiat reward, a USD stablecoin in a cash book). PLAN step 14 moved them from the crypto parsers. The two exports' rows differ, so each has its own legs: Kraken's ledger credit valued from its USD amounts (`ledger_reward_legs`: the DIVIDEND in USD, its income net of a commission charged in another currency, a stablecoin in a property book at par, the acquisition with no gross amount), its caller reading the amounts and passing the stablecoin model (`src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/kraken.py` — `_income_row`); and Coinbase's row valued in its price currency, the value chosen from the cells `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/coinbase.py` — `_build_staking` reads, a cell that does not fit refused through the parser's own identity and tolerances (`_check_qp`, `_QP_TOL_ABS`), both legs at that value with the reward's evidence (`_staking_evidence`) on the DIVIDEND. A negative reward is each parser's refusal.
- `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/assets.py` — `FIAT_CURRENCIES`, `USD_STABLECOINS`, `_LiveStablecoins`, `QTY_ZERO`: what an asset code is to the books, one answer for the parsers and the crypto readers (the fiat currencies; the USD stablecoins, read at each use with the ticker.map STABLE lines; the books' zero for a traded quantity). PLAN step 14 moved them from lib/brokerages so the toolbox imports nothing from it; `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/_crypto_common.py` and `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/schema.py` re-export them for lib's readers.
- `src/taxjson/parsers/conformance.py` — `check`, `account_phase_findings` (each stage of the account phase takes what taxjson-brokerage passes it, `ACCOUNT_CALLS`), `detection_findings` (the router the caller hands, `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/detect.py` — `detect`, sends the fixture to the parser), `country_findings` (under each country's settings the fixture parses, passes the schema and leaves no row unaccounted; then the identical rows, `_identical_rows_findings`: at each basis, `bases`, the rows, counts and skips by class under each country are those under no country, and a basis field the parser does not name in its `BASIS_INPUTS` changes none of them, `_outcome_of`; `_parse_under`; what it cannot see, its docstring says), `basis_effects` (the basis fields a fixture's parse depends on: a declared field none of a parser's fixtures needs is stale, tests/test_parser_check.py), `read_fixture_meta` (the folder's `fixture.toml`: the expected skip count, the parser a second export's folder is for, the kinds it claims, `CLAIMS`, `_claim`, and the account settings its parses are told, `FIXTURE_SETTINGS`: the one place the suite and the command read them), `fixture_settings` (those settings, which `parse_fixture` and `_parse_under` tell every parse), `kind_findings` (every row and cash event of a kind, each row's declared by its parser, each claimed kind had), `declared_findings` (each kind a parser declares claimed by one of its folders), `cash_events_of` (the fixture's cash events, the parse's own, a finding when a broker whose parse builds them built none), `unclaimed_note` (what `tjs parser-check` says of a folder that claims no kinds), `fixture_parser_id`, `fixture_dirs` (a parser's folders: its own and the ones naming it), `parse_fixture`, `schema_findings`, `golden_findings`, `CASH_GOLDEN`, `cash_golden_findings` (the folder's `expected_cash.json`: the cash events against their golden, `_golden_findings` the one comparison), `determinism_findings`, `accounting_findings`: the conformance checks every parser passes on a fixture folder, asserted per shipped parser by `tests/parser_conformance.py` and run for any registered parser by `src/taxjson/cli/maintainer.py` — `cmd_parser_check` (`tjs parser-check`), `_dev_checkout` (the checkout a maintainer command reads, a wrong TAXJSON_DEV_DIR refused in its own words).
- `src/taxjson/lib/privacy.py` — `mask_id`: how a message prints a broker's identifier (its first two characters and `***`), re-exported by `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/masking.py` for the parsers (step 5a of the engine refactor; each parser names its own fallback for an empty value).
- `src/taxjson/lib/privacy.py` — `mask_id`, `mask_ids` (`_IB_ID_RE`, `_DIGITS_RE`, `_DATE_RE`): the one implementation of the privacy rule for identifiers (TJ-2063, TJ-2352): an id's first two characters and `***`, and every account-id-shaped token inside a file name or a message (an IB account id with its prefix, a digit run of 7 to 12 that is not a date; the base module's shown_name reads it for a row's source name); the helpers lib, pipeline and bin kept (`positions_reports.mask_account`, `holdings_dir.mask`, `sanity._mask_ids_in_path`, `corp_actions._mask_account`, `missing_history._mask_id`, `crypto_sends.mask_ref`) call it.
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `BaseBrokerage`, `pair_warrant_legs` (a warrant exercise's share leg and its pairing, by root across the day's warrants, one copy for RBC and IB: tests/test_one_copy.py), `rename_reach`, `rename_how` (whether a listing looks renamed with no corporate-action row, and how the new one goes short: Questrade's and RBC's rule, one copy; Webull's broader rule is its own), `parse_file`, `count_skip`, `emit_skip_summary`, `format_occ_symbol`, `apply_currency_suffix`, `canonical_ca_listing`, `is_ca_pref_series` (a dotted preferred series keeps its L or V), `read_broker_text`, `equity_settlement_date`, `income_facts_from_description`, `set_ticker_map`, `ticker_map_mentioned`, `set_declared_renames`, `declared_rename_where`, `source_identity`, `note_row_line`, `rows_noting_lines`, `check_account_plans`, `check_account_plans_across`, `plan_shelter_of`, `plan_taxed_of`, `is_taxable_account_type`, `taxed_plans_note`, `REGISTERED_PLAN_RE` (an export's account types against the taxjson account, several broker accounts in one file or across an account's files said or refused: one copy for Questrade's Account Type and the generic importer's `account_type` column, whose exports' accounts the generic importer reads before the parses, tests/test_one_copy.py): the base class and its row-accounting contract (and each row's export line, for the schema check's messages); option and listing symbols; text reading (the strict number parsers and the parse error are the toolbox's since step 5a, re-exported here); settlement dates and income facts; what a parser knows of ticker.map and of the .tt RENAME lines, and of which account a file belongs to.
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/schema.py` — `SCHEMA`, `KNOWN_ACTIONS`, `validate_transactions`, `render_schema_prompt`: the normalized-row schema and its validator.

## Equity broker parsers

One parser per broker export. Each reads the broker's activity CSV and emits
BUY, SELL, DIVIDEND, TAX, ROC, SPLIT, TRANSFER and the other actions in the
schema. Account-level context (positions over time, reversals, ticker
changes) is built across all of an account's files before rows are emitted.

- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/questrade.py` — `_qt_real_order` (a newest-first export's rows in real order, for the parse and its cash events), `_row_clock` (a row's own date and clock, the one rule for every row kind; a cash in lieu's sale takes its parent's and follows it), `_credits_before_reinvestments`, `_pays_reinvestment` (a reinvestment follows the cash that pays it: a dividend or return of capital of its security, or an interest credit, at its clock is ordered before it, and a reinvestment at the export's midnight paid by a return of capital takes that row's fixed 09:30:00; no real clock is lent; for the cash ledgers), `QuestradeBrokerage` on the parse template since step 5h (4): `read` (the account context, the rows in book order as `_QtRow` objects, the taxable hint, the account mix; what the later phases need as `_QtParse`; every row seen), `classify` (the header and an FX conversion non-events, a cash row noted for the file's cash events, `_qt_cash_kind`, the currency, then the chain by action code, the builder kept on the row), `build` (one builder per branch, the row's account stamped on what it books: `_build_split`, `_build_stock_dividend`, `_build_dividend`, `_build_transfer`, `_build_fee`, `_build_interest`, `_build_cil`, `_build_reinvestment`, `_build_journal`, `_build_trade` over `_trade_shape` and `_trade_money`, their facts `_QtShape` and `_QtMoney`), `finish` (the reversals paired, the notes, the file's cash events: `_cash_events_of_rows`, the FXT legs paired in the rows' real order, over `_cash_amounts` and `_cash_day`, whose refusal is the file's `cash_problem`, PLAN step 16 commit 9), `after_marks` (the journal pairs costed once the marks saw the legs as the loop made them), `release`; `QtAccountContext`, `build_qt_account_context`, `_plan_qt_reversals`, `_plan_qt_positions` (each broker account's position, which tells a short's split from a reverse split), `_detect_qt_ticker_changes`, `scan_code_uses`, `_plan_qt_journals`, `_journal_listing`, `journal_codes`: Questrade activity exports, including internal security codes and BRW currency journals (paired legs, the US-dollar line, codes learned from a journal leg). `blank_symbol_twins`, `_warn_blank_symbol_twins`: a file's rows that differ only by a blank Symbol (one trade an older `tjs fetch` kept twice), said as one ATTENTION per file naming the pairs' lines (`_QT_TWINS_SHOWN` of them, the rest counted), in the end-of-run list too; nothing dropped.
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/rbc_direct.py` — `RbcBrokerage`, `read_rbc_rows`, `classify_rbc_row`, `RbcAccountContext`, `build_rbc_account_context`, `_plan_reinvest_reversals`, `rbc_coverage_messages`, `is_holdings_export`, `RbcFormatError`, `rbc_cash_move`, `rbc_trade_side`, `rbc_desc_side`, `_checked_trade_side`, `_refuse_trade_side`, `_refuse_side_against_value`: RBC Direct Investing; every row is classified by its activity label and event code (a trade's direction from its Activity, or from its Description's Buy / Sell under an Activity not known; the two contradicting refused, and a sale that pays, or a direction read from the Description that the sign of the cash contradicts: the Value where the export has the Amount too; with one money column, a sale read from the Description that pays Quantity x Price or more, a Sell row left to the money check); date coverage, holdings files and format refusals; the file's cash events built from the cash rows it read (PLAN step 16 commit 7). Every lookup by company name (temporary codes, listings, spin-off parents) reads the name through `src/taxjson/lib/corp_actions.py` — `rbc_row_company` (the optional Symbol Description, else the trade's own Description).
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/ib_extractor.py` — `IbBrokerage` on the parse template since step 5h (8): `read` (the statement's state set up, the prescan and the account's aliases, its rows as `_IbRow` records with the header map in force), `new_transactions` (the template's list is the statement's: a `Ca` row undoes rows built before it), `classify` (the loop's checks: a row type IB does not write, a row cut short, an unknown section), `build` (the section's method), `finish`, `release` (the rows' state), `emit_skip_summary` (deferred to the account's pass under an account context); `_IbParse` (one statement's parse state on `self._ib`, kept after the parse: what crosses statements, `ca_seen` to `unmatched_refunds`, which `_reconcile_files` reads as `ex._ib.<field>`), `ca_undo`, `cil_join`, `_ib_ca_record`, `_ib_ca_apply_undo`, `_ib_book`, (the parse's methods over its state, parse_file's closures until 5h (8a); tests/test_ib_state.py holds that read binds no field's name and that no field is rebound), `_flush_ca_side_effects` (bound to its statement's state when deferred), `_IbRows` (the rows' shared state, on `self._ib_rows` from read to release: the containers by one name, the cash booked per Cash Report line, the header maps), `_ROW_METHODS` (the loop's dispatch by section), `_ib_finish` (the end of the parse, 5h (8c): `_ib_finish_left_cancels`, `_ib_finish_trade_cancels`, `_ib_finish_refunds`, `_ib_finish_unmatched_ca`, `_ib_finish_unmatched_cil`, `_ib_finish_tenders`, `_ib_finish_dividend_facts` (a Dividends row's accrual: `_ib_rows_paid` over `_ib_accrual_for`; the rows of a pay date two distributions share matched to them by the statement's evidence, `_ib_tied`, `_ib_tied_ways` (the ways weighed: whole shares, the same shares lent, the most accruals with rows of their own), `_ib_explains`, refused where it leaves the match open, `_ib_tied_refusal`, `_ib_tied_head`; a reading the weighing set aside that gives other shares or rates said in an ATTENTION, `_ib_way_shares`, `_ib_way_said`, `_ib_whole_reading`), `_ib_finish_open_accruals`, `_ib_finish_income` (an income row still on the listing its ISIN's country alone gave, a domicile and no market, `_isin_ext`: booked on evidence of the listing (the account's holding, a ticker.map line naming the symbol, the instrument list's one venue) or refused saying what the statement shows, `_ib_settle_assumed_listings`, `_ib_isin_venues`, `_ib_held_as`, `_ib_income_kinds`, `_ib_assumed_listing_refusal`), `_ib_finish_assignments`, `_ib_finish_cash`: the statement's cash events, PLAN step 16 commit 10, from the rows read noted as the export holds them, before its temporary symbols are folded, `_ib_cash_rows` (each an `_IbCashRow`: the accounts it names, the period, the Forex fills of its detail level, `_ib_cash_order_level`, the Deposits & Withdrawals, Cash Report and Forex Balances rows), built in their order by `_ib_cash_events_of` over `_ib_forex_fill`, `_ib_deposit_move`, `_ib_forex_events` (a `Ca` pair no conversion, `_ib_forex_cancels`) and `_ib_cash_close`), `_ib_same_account`, `_ib_other_statement`, `_ib_join_cash_in_lieu` (the cross-statement pass's cash-in-lieu step: entries from live copies; `_ib_cil_key`, `_ib_cil_cancelled`), the cross-statement pass's other steps, in `_reconcile_files`' order (step 5's cut: no parser function over 300 lines): `_ib_cross_corp_cas` (Corporate Actions `Ca` rows; `_ib_ca_key`), `_ib_cross_trade_cas`, `_ib_cross_held_cas` (the Trades and Transfers `Ca` rows a parse held), `_ib_cross_xfer_cas`, `_ib_cross_refunds` (commission refunds), over `_ib_same_acct` and `_ib_nm`, `_ib_row_open_positions`, `_ib_row_statement`, `_ib_row_accruals`, `_ib_row_withholding`, `_ib_row_interest`, `_ib_row_fees`, `_ib_row_transaction_fees`, `_ib_row_commission_adjustments`, `_ib_row_dividends` (its return of capital `_ib_dividend_roc`: a foreign issuer's marked for the characterize step), `_ib_row_transfers` (its cancellation pairing `_ib_transfer_cancel_pair`), `_ib_row_trades` (its row's record `_IbTrade`, which each step fills and reads: `_ib_trade_other_category`, `_ib_trade_level`, `_ib_trade_dates`, `_ib_trade_kind`, `_ib_trade_symbol`, `_ib_trade_book`), `_ib_row_corporate_actions` (its row's record `_IbCa`, with the effect a `Ca` undoes and `listing_ext`: `_ib_ca_cancel`, then `_ib_ca_event` by kind: `_ib_ca_tender`, `_ib_ca_cil`, `_ib_ca_split` (a split carrying cash refused, its fix: `_ib_split_cash_refusal`), `_ib_ca_cash_merger`, `_ib_ca_stock_dividend`; `_ib_ca_nonevent`, `_ib_ca_unbooked`) (one section's Data row each, 5h (8b), answering what the row is, a parsers.protocol `Outcome`, that the parse counts (5h (8d); a Corporate Actions row's through `_ib_ca_settle`, after its effect is recorded); tests/test_ib_state.py holds that every path answers, that no row counts itself and that `read` binds no `_IbRows` field's name as a local), `_ib_accrual_index`, `_ib_accrual_for`, `_ib_pay_distance`, `prepare_files`, `reconcile_files`, `resolve_unmatched_ca`, `ib_year_coverage`, `get_ib_settlement`, `_ib_market_trade_date`, `_ib_futures_trade_date`, `_ib_xfer_cancels`, `_ib_fold_refund`, `_ib_temp_folds`, `_map_names_temp`, `_project_map_names`, `_ib_fold_rows`, `_warn_stock_aliases`, `_security_name`, `_reattribute_income_to_holdings`, `_listing_replay`, `_ib_share_stated_settle`, `_ib_number_executions`, `_ib_number_group`: Interactive Brokers activity statements (every section of one CSV; a row's security name from the instrument on its own listing's market when several share its symbol; an ISIN-named income row on the listing held on its date, by a replay of trades, transfers, splits and ticker changes in clock order, the account's other statements' listings, as names, for a statement with none: two such listings leave the row on its ISIN listing, noted as "may have held"; a cost row never moved onto a listing the statement's own rows show not held that day); coverage gaps, settlement and trade dates (a futures fill by its venue's session: `src/taxjson/lib/markets.py` — `ib_futures_session`), cancellations and fee refunds; IB's temporary time-stamped symbols folded onto their ticker (unless a ticker.map line names the stamped symbol), and one contract under two symbols (a dated RENAME hint). The listing suffix it gives a row (a currency's; a stock's or warrant's: a USD unit of a TSX-listed fund is a Canadian listing, a line on the LSE is `.L`, an unknown venue keeps the currency's suffix, noted once) and the currency/venue tags of a dotted symbol, which lib/corp_actions reads too: `src/taxjson/lib/ib_listing.py` — `_ib_currency_ext`, `_ib_listing_ext`, `_IB_CURRENCY_TAGS`.
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/webull.py` — `WebullBrokerage` on the parse template since step 5h (5): `read` (the Trading Summary header found, the preamble's account, the columns; the rows a generator, `_rows`, each a `_WbRow` with its line; what the later phases need as `_WbParse`), `classify` (a preamble's account line a segment boundary, the page headers and preamble rows non-events, a non-trade action a skip kept for the warning), `build` (the trade over `_trade_facts`; its Type Code read as its kind, `_TYPE_KINDS`, checked against the description, another code refused: `_check_type_code`), `finish` (`_file_rows`: the row accounting, each segment oldest first before the marks; then the folder's sibling exports, each a `_sibling` of this parser with its settings and `pool_hints` off, pooled through `parse_rows` for the assignment, zero-close and ticker-change hints), `release`; `label_hits`, `_WEBULL_OPTION_RE`, `_deliverable_size`: Webull exports (both column layouts, matched by header label).
- `src/taxjson/lib/trade_cancel.py` — `pair_cancellations`, `is_trade_cancel`, `TRADE_CANCEL_TYPE`, `trade_cancel_what`: an IB `Ca` cancellation netted against its original (a partial one reduces it; later ones match what is left; a cancellation of the whole order after one of its executions' removes it in full: `overlaps`).
- `src/taxjson/lib/futures_prefixes.py` — `FUTURES_PREFIXES`, `FUTURES_PREFIX_GROUP` (a regex group of them), `FUTURES_PREFIX_RE` (a symbol's leading prefix; `src/taxjson/lib/option_symbols.py` — `_FUTURES_PREFIX_RE` reads it): taxjson's futures symbol prefixes (`F:`, `/`, `\`), their one copy, a dependency-free leaf every module reads (`tests/test_one_copy.py` holds every module to it; lib/option_symbols.py `_OCC_OPTION_RE` and lib/futures.py `_FUTURE_ROOT_RE` are built from the group). `canonical_futures_symbol`, `BOOK_FUTURES_PREFIX`: the one fold of a future written `/` or `\` (an option on one included) to the books' `F:` (`FuturesSymbolError`: one that names no contract once folded, `//QZM26.US`, `/F:QZM26.US`, `/.US`, is refused, each reader naming its line), which every reader of a symbol a person writes calls: the .tt reader (`src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `_book_symbol`, `_canonical_ca_symbols`, so `compute_tt_id` hashes the `F:` spelling), a ticker.map or tobase.map line (`src/taxjson/lib/ca_listing.py` — `book_listing`; `src/taxjson/lib/ticker_map.py` — `parse_side_line`, `extract_symbol`), a holdings file (`src/taxjson/lib/positions_reports.py` — `_read_toml`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/sanity.py` — `_file_symbol`; `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_export.py` — `_holdings_toml_to_inventory`), a loss override (`src/taxjson/lib/loss_overrides.py` — `load_file`; `src/taxjson/lib/loss_override_note.py` — `parse_line`), a filed year's dispositions (`src/taxjson/lib/handoff.py` — `load_filed_dispositions`), a slip (`src/taxjson/lib/slip_symbols.py` — `slip_symbol`), the generic importer (`src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/generic.py` — `_listing_symbol`), a JSON book a tool reads (`src/taxjson/lib/tx_load.py` — `fold_row_symbols`, `coerce_transaction_row`: a stated id kept, a computed one hashing `F:`; TJ-5160; `taxjson-convert-tt` writes it: `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `_json_book_rows`), the capital-gains dividends (`src/taxjson/lib/cg_dividends.py` — `_book_sym`, `parse_map`, `entries_from_config`), a closed year's record as every lock reader reads it (`src/taxjson/lib/filed_locks.py` — `fold_lock_symbols`, `load_lock_json`: an older taxjson's `/` record matches this year's `F:` books, a security under both spellings merged) and the commands' symbol arguments (`src/taxjson/cli/plan.py` — `_fold_class_separator`; `src/taxjson/cli/summaries.py` — `_view_positionals`; a refusal is `src/taxjson/lib/cli_diag.py` — `guard_main`'s one line) (backlog TJ-5150).
