- `src/taxjson/lib/futures.py` — `settle_futures_dicts`, `fee_basis`, `_own_fee`; `src/taxjson/lib/cash_legs.py` — `_cash_legs`: ledger v2 dates each futures commission on its own fill.

## Crypto parsers

Kraken and Coinbase exports are read strictly: amounts must parse exactly, and
local times need a time zone. Withdrawals and deposits are kept as TRANSFER
custody evidence for the crypto sends step.

- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/kraken.py` — `KrakenBrokerage` on the parse template since step 5h (7): `read` (the export's kind from its header line, the row readers' assets set for the parse and restored by `release`, the kind's state as `_KrParse`; the rows a generator, each a `_KrRow`), `classify` (`_classify_trade`: the duplicate-id check, a type neither buy nor sell a skip; `_classify_ledger`: a repeated row a non-event, the row's time, amount and asset read, then the type chain), `build` (`_build_fill` over `_fill_facts` / `_KrFill` then `_build_pair` or `_build_fiat_trade`; `_build_income`, `_build_ledger_transfer`, `_build_fiat_funding`), `finish` (`_finish_trades`: the notes and the order within a second before the template's marks; `_finish_ledgers`: the notes, the instant trades paired, the zero-transactions reason; then `_finish_cash`, a ledgers export's cash events from the rows `_classify_ledger` noted, below), `release`; `KrakenAccountContext`, `prepare_files`, `_join_instant_legs`, `header_kind`, `REQUIRED_BY_KIND`, `known_assets_of`, `_ledger_identity`
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/kraken.py` — `_kr_walk`, `_kr_entries`, `_kr_listing`, `_kr_set_listing`, `_kr_listed`, `_kr_frac`, `_kr_said`, `_KrEntry`, `_KrWalk`, `_kr_place`, `_kr_taken`, `_kr_orders`, `_kr_result`, `_kr_across`, `_KrUnordered`, `_KR_UNSURE`: the account's ledger rows walked in time order, a wallet at a time, for the cash events (`_kr_cash_of`, `_kr_cash_balances`) and the parse's Hybrid Earn lines (`_finish_hybrid`, `_hybrid_unordered`, `_hybrid_left`). The order the export gives the rows of one second (`_kr_listed`) is its lines, read from the last up where it lists the wallet's rows newest first (`_kr_listing`, `_kr_set_listing`, `_KrEntry.seq`: told from every pair of the wallet's rows that follow each other with different times, never from the first and the last row; an export whose rows of the wallet go both ways in time, or share one time, states no direction), or, where each row states a fraction of the second (`_kr_frac`), the order of those. The rows of one wallet in one second are put in the order the `balance` column gives when it settles one (`_kr_taken`): every row of the second chained from the wallet's balance before it (the balance after the second before, or one of that second's balances where its own order is not known) or to the balance the next second begins at, or, with no balance on either side, among themselves, with one result whichever such chain is taken, and that chain either the file's order or anchored on both sides with every balance stated (the rows then account for the whole difference between the two balances); a listed order that chains to where the next second's listed first row begins is taken whatever else chains, where the export states its direction (a second that comes back to the balance it began at, every month); otherwise (no chain, chains that disagree, a chain that is not the file's order with a balance on one side only or none: a row may be missing inside the second or before it) the file's order is kept, as before the balances were read, its rows marked `unsure` (so are the rows of a chain taken that is not sure: one read down or up where the export states no direction, or one the stated fractions contradict; and every row after a second of Hybrid Earn rows whose order is not known), which a CASHBAL read from them says in its `where` (and its `unsure`: a gap line of the ledger quotes the `where`, and a balance that reconciles is a Warning, `src/taxjson/lib/fx_cash_v2.py` — `_unsure`, `warned`: the end-of-run note and the `tjs sum` line say how many warnings the ledger has). A CASHBAL is the balance after the last row in that order, so an export listed newest first ends a second at its last row. Kraken Hybrid Earn rows (`_HYBRID_EARN`, `_kr_is_hybrid`, `_kr_hybrid_step`: `hybridearnwithdrawal` out of the spot balance, `hybridearndeposit` back, no row of another wallet) are a move within Kraken: the cash events have a CASHMOVE for neither direction but a PARKED event (`_kr_parked_event`; `src/taxjson/parsers/protocol.py` — `CASH_KINDS`), each wallet's balance adds what its own rows up to the balance row used had parked (`_KrWalk.parked_at`, its `where` saying how much), and what a row brings back beyond what was parked is not booked as income or as coins acquired: an ATTENTION line per asset and, for cash, an undeclared CASHMOVE of the excess (`_kr_cash_own`). A second holding Hybrid Earn rows that is read in the file's order while another order the balances allow gives another result is said in an ATTENTION line of its own (backlog TJ-4951, TJ-4954).
- `src/taxjson/lib/cash_events.py` — `apply_declarations`, `refused_lines`, `parked_lines`, `parked_text`, `parked_fix`, `_mark_rows`, `_rows_of`, `_note_copies`, `send_decisions`, `send_conflict_text`, `SEND_HOW`, `STILL_YOURS`: what declares a broker's cash move, for the ledger (`src/taxjson/lib/fx_cash_v2.py` — `_declarations`) and `tjs cash` (`src/taxjson/cli/listings.py` — `cmd_cash`) alike: a `.tt` CASHMOVE line that fits it; else, for a crypto account's stablecoin send, the decision `tjs crypto-sends` saved (`src/taxjson/lib/crypto_sends.py` — `cash_send_decisions`: a gift or a payment `spot`, a `self` send `kept`; sends alike that day with different decisions give none), a line of the other side refused with the decision (backlog TJ-4953). A line that declares no broker move and fits a Kraken Hybrid Earn row of cash (a PARKED event) declares cash that never moved: refused by the ledger in the year it is dated in, a Warning in a later year's ledger, left out of `tjs cash`; the lines are set against the moves and the PARKED events together, so a line that fits both declares neither and is refused with every line alike and both rows named, until a line names its row (`row=`, bare or in double quotes where the file's name holds a space; `src/taxjson/lib/cash_lines.py` — `_ROW`, `_ROW_QUOTED`, `row_token`, `parse_line`), in any of the exports that hold the row (`_note_copies`); the `?` line of `tjs cash` (`tt_line`) and the line the ledger asks for a move that a Hybrid Earn row is alike with carry that `row=` (`row_needed`), and a line of an earlier year that the ledger of its year refuses is a Warning (`src/taxjson/lib/fx_cash_v2.py` — `_asked`, `_earlier_year`) (backlog TJ-4951).
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/kraken.py` — `_kr_wallet`, `_KR_NAMED`, `_kr_join_unnamed`, `_KR_SUFFIX_KIND`, `_kr_cash_balances`, `_kr_wallet_at`, `_kr_one_listing`, `_kr_uncovered`, `_kr_wallet_gaps`, `_kr_is_move`, `_KR_OUT_OF_BOOK`, `_kr_note`, `_kr_mixed_note`, `_kr_cash_own`, `_kr_cash_conversions`, `_kr_leg`: the account's cash balances and the ids of its cash events. A wallet is its asset code with its suffix and the wallet the export names in its `wallet` column; rows of an export without the column join the asset's one named wallet of their kind (a bare code the spot wallet, `.F` the flexible Earn wallet, by the suffix table), are a wallet of their own where no export names one of that kind, and are said in the balance (`unsure`) where two named wallets of the kind leave it open (backlog TJ-5027). A CASHBAL is the ACCOUNT's balance, the same from every export that holds a balance row of cash: each wallet's balance at the date over every ledger export read (the walk's rows: the union of the exports' rows, an entry of one txid once, `_kr_entries`, the rows of one wallet in one second read as the one export that holds the most of them lists them, `_kr_one_listing`, so every export walks the same rows in the same order and derives the same Hybrid Earn excess: backlog TJ-5224), the wallets of a currency added up, at the end of each year the account's rows span, at their last day and before their first row; its `where` names the exports whose rows were read. A wallet's balance at a date is the one after its latest row, or, where the next row that states a balance begins at another one than that and the blank-balance rows between give (rows are missing between two exports), the one that next row begins at (the CASHBAL carries `opens`, and the ledger's gap line says it as the later export's opening: `src/taxjson/lib/fx_cash_v2.py` — `_gap_text`); before its first row, the balance that row begins at, known from the year end just before it (earlier, a currency with such a wallet gets no balance and a NOTE, unless the row begins at nothing). A currency with a wallet move (`_KrEntry.move`: an `earn` allocation, a legacy transfer between the spot and the staking wallet, neither a CASHMOVE: backlog TJ-5227; not a transfer to or from futures, which leaves the book as a CASHMOVE) whose other side no export read holds gets no balance from that day on, and a NOTE naming the row (backlog TJ-5032). A cash move, its fee and a Hybrid Earn row's events carry the row's txid as `_broker_id`, and a trade's conversion, spread and fees its refid with `:conv`, `:spread` or `:fee:<the leg's txid>` (backlog TJ-5033), read by `src/taxjson/lib/cash_events.py` — `dedup_across_files`, `_id_key`, `_id_kept`, `_id_differs`, `_id_conflicts`, `_ID_CONTENT`, `CashIdConflict`, `id_conflicts`, `_export_events`: the events of one book, kind and broker's id are one event where their content agrees (the date, the currencies, the amounts to the cent; the kept copy takes the other copies' rows, `_line_also`; an event more of the id that one file alone holds is kept), and an id two files hold with different content is refused, one line per id, naming both rows, the masked id and the field (an amount the parse derives, `_derived`, said as the exports listing a second's rows differently; backlog TJ-5034). `tjs run` says each as an ATTENTION and stops with --strict (`src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_check_cash_ids`), the checklist's run step lists it (`src/taxjson/lib/checklist.py` — `_cash_id_conflicts`), and the commands that read the cash events exit 2 before any output (`src/taxjson/cli/common.py` — `_refuse_cash_older_than_inputs`, `_die_cash_ids`; `src/taxjson/cli/listings.py` — `cmd_cash`); the in-process readers of the ledger (`src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_fx_cash_v2_doc`) carry the text as the ledger's refusal (backlog TJ-5221). Tests: `tests/features/parsers/test_kraken_account_balances_and_event_ids.py` — `TestAWalletIsItsCodeAndItsName`, `TestTheBalanceIsTheAccounts`, `TestCashEventsCarryTheLedgersIds`; `tests/rules/ca/fx/test_foreign_cash_one_broker_id_two_contents.py` — `TestOneIdTwoContents`, `TestTheCommandsRefuseIt`.
- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/coinbase.py` — `CoinbaseBrokerage` on the parse template since step 5h (6): `read` (the export decoded; the rows a generator, `_rows`, each a `_CbRow` with the line it starts and the line it ends on, the header found in the stream, the rows before it and the blank lines dropped; what the later phases need as `_CbParse`), `classify` (the shape checks, the row ids checked for duplicates, the type chain: a Convert, a Send or Receive and a Buy / Sell / reward build, a known type a non-event, another a skip noted as unbooked), `build` (`_build_convert_row` over `_build_convert`, `_build_transfer`, `_build_row` with its `_CbFacts` then `_build_staking` with `_staking_evidence` or `_build_trade`), `finish` (the blank Totals note, the unbooked warning, the file's cash events: `_cash_events_of_rows` over the rows classify noted, `note_cash`, each by `_cash_events_of_row`, a Convert's stablecoin leg `_convert_cash_leg`, a fiat or cash-coin row's `_currency_row_cash`, a record's `rec` its `where`; a cash cell it cannot read the file's `cash_problem`, PLAN step 16 commit 8), `after_marks` (`order_within_seconds` after the template's fill marks, which mark the id-less rows alone), `release`; `resolve_header`, `is_header_row`, `_cb_symbol`, `_HEADER_SYNONYMS`, `coinbase_row_total` (a row's Total, a blank one derived, for the cash events and the crypto-sends pool): Coinbase transaction exports.
- `src/taxjson/lib/timezones.py` — `utc_to_local`, `LocalTimezoneMissing`, `system_timezone` (the local zone crypto rows are dated in); `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/_crypto_common.py` — `usd_value`, `warn_depeg`, `RowIds`: helpers both crypto parsers share (`RowIds`: one broker id on two different rows of a file is refused).
- `src/taxjson/bin/fill_crypto_prices.py` — `main`, `_fill`, `_implausible_yahoo_prices`: the `taxjson-fill-crypto` stage that prices crypto rows with no fiat value; `_damaged_entry` (a cached 0, negative or non-number is a miss, looked up again); `_swap_pairs`, `_value_swaps_once` (a swap's two legs take one value: a stablecoin leg's par, else the received coin's close); `_swap_fee_factor` (a fee the parser put on the legs as `swap_fee`, added to a value taken from the received coin). The lookup and the cache in $HOME it reads and writes: `src/taxjson/lib/crypto_prices.py` — `get_crypto_price`, `yahoo_id`, `load_cache`, `save_cache`, `cached_price`, `CACHE_FILE` (bin reads them through the module, so a test's patch of `CACHE_FILE` there keeps the real cache out).

## Generic CSV, hand entry (.tt) and adding a parser

A broker with no parser can be read through a column mapping (`generic_*.csv`
plus its mapping). Hand-entered rows go in `.tt` text files (one transaction
per line), which `tjs run` converts to JSON. A new parser registers in
`taxjson_brokerage.py`, adds a detector, and passes the conformance harness
against a synthetic fixture.

- `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/generic.py` — `GenericBrokerage` on the parse template since step 5h (3): `prepare_files` (`_GenAccountContext`, `_account_cells`: the account's exports' broker accounts and types, read before the parses for the plan check across them, TJ-2584), `read` (the mapping resolved and checked, the file decoded, its header checked; what the later phases need as `_GenParse`; the rows a generator, `_rows`, each a `_GenRow` with its line, so a malformed record is refused in file order), `classify` (`_classify_row`; the repeated header a non-event and a segment boundary), `build` (the row's `_RowFacts`, then `_build_trade` over `_trade_money`, `_build_assign` (an `assign_buy` / `assign_sell` leg, `_ASSIGN_SIDE` its direction, through `_build_trade` as an ASSIGN row; the finish's `_check_assign_legs` pairs the file's legs through core `_pair_assign_legs`, gives an option leg its share leg's settle date, refuses a side the share legs contradict, and leaves the claimed legs out of `_warn_assignment_shapes`), `_build_event`, `_build_income`; an Outcome for the non-event an event builder finds), `finish` (the pasted overlap, the sign conventions, the notes, then `_oldest_first`: each newest-first segment reversed, before the template's fill marks), `release`; `effective_mapping` (the mapping a CSV is read with: the sidecar, the folder's generic.toml for a generic_ file or by its `[signature]`, a layout by its signature; `MappingChoice` with the files the choice depends on), `layout_dirs_of`, `layout_dirs` (the project's folder through `project_of_input`, the user's through `user_config_dir`) and `layout_files` (a name's first folder shadows the next's), `BUILTIN_LAYOUTS` (the layouts taxjson ships, `src/taxjson/data/layouts/`, the last folder: `is_builtin_layout`, weighed in `_effective_mapping` only when no project or user layout fits; `shipped_layout_hint`, where an edited copy goes, on an UNBOOKED row; the first, Wealthsimple's, step 5's layout broker, its fixture `tests/fixtures/wealthsimple`), `_layout_file`, `_candidate_mappings`, `_signed_mappings` (the file read only when a candidate has a signature; a bad layout `LayoutError` at detection), `_first_records` (the first 20 records as the parse's reader splits them, those before a record it refuses), `_head_records` (them from the first 64 KB cut at a line break, read once for every signature), `HEADER_WITHIN` and `header_index` (the header: the first of the first 20 records holding every mapped name, or a signature's; None for none), `_header_reader` (the parse's and `_account_cells`' reader past the lines above it; `line_offset` turns its line numbers into the file's; `_refuse_rows_above`: a record above it that reads as data refused), `preamble_records` (those records, which `tjs redact` blanks), `mapping_bytes` (the drift check's fast path), `_named_layout`, `_choice_state` and `_cached_choice` (the answer cached on the files' state), `MappingChoice.refused` (the lenient fallback), `mapping_documents` (the mapping and its layout as documents, the frozen year's drift check), `MappingAmbiguity`, `mapping_path`, `_read_mapping_file`, `_check_signature`, `_load_mapping` (a `[layout]` read in the file's stead, its `[broker]` keys winning), `_check_keys`, `_check_actions`, `_check_targets` (the column each mapped target books from, required at load time), `_load_classify` and `_load_extract` (the `[[classify]]` rules and the `[extract]` patterns compiled at load time, `_regex`; `[extract] option`'s named groups `_OPTION_GROUPS`, made the row's OCC symbol by `_option_symbol` through base.py's `option_parts` and the template's `format_occ_symbol`, `_option_beside_ticker` the contract or the shares when the symbol cell holds a ticker), `_Cells` (a file's mapped cells as the loop reads them: the column's text, else the pulled value; a refusal names the source), `_FIELDS_OF` (the fields each target books, which a pattern may fill), `_classify_row` (a row's label, target and description: the rules after `[actions]`, then the target's patterns pulled into the overlay `cell` reads), `_check_trade_row`, `_event_row` (the event targets' dispatcher: every event names its symbol), `_split_row` (a split or rename row as one SPLIT row), `_RowCells` (a row's mapped cells for the event builders), `_transfer_row` (`transfer_in` / `transfer_out`: the direction the target's, the cost evidence opt-in through `book_value` or `market_value`, which may name the amount's header; an amount left unread is noted), `_opening_row` (through `lib/opening.opening_row`; its `lot_date` in `_day_formats`; an option's size the ticker.map's), `_adjust_row` (`roc` through the base class's `tx_roc_adjust`), `_note_unbooked` (an unmapped row that moves shares, cash, a ratio or a ticker): the column-mapped importer.
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_convert_tt.py` — `main` (a book that is not valid JSON or not UTF-8 exits 2, a line or row it cannot convert 1: backlog TJ-5154), `_main` (the date-basis check reads the rows through `src/taxjson/lib/tx_load.py` — `read_book_rows`, backlog TJ-5151); `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `tt_to_json`, `parse_tt_line`, `parse_opening_line`, `json_to_tt_lines`, `compute_tt_id`: the .tt format in both directions (a JSON book read and checked by the shared loader, `_json_book_rows`: a trade row with no amount refused, backlog TJ-5035); `tt_lines`: a .tt file's lines, split at a line feed only, for every reader of one; `tt_tail`, `size_text`, `EXERCISE_KEY`: the tokens at the end of a line (a contract size, a warrant exercise's `exercise_of=`, an income row's facts), written alike by `tx_to_tt_line` and the `tjs events` view (`src/taxjson/cli/listings.py` — `_tx_display_line`); `_refuse_twice`: a `key=` token given twice is refused; `tx_tt_date`, `PAID_DATE_ACTIONS`: the one date a line carries (an income row's the day it was paid, backlog TJ-0862); `INTEREST_SYMBOL_KEY`, `_interest_tokens`: an INTEREST line's property (backlog TJ-0856); `CASH_LEG_KEY`, `REFUNDS_KEY`, `_CASH_KEYS`, `_cash_tokens`, `_check_cash_keys`, `_cash_leg_problem`: the cash facts the FX-on-cash ledgers read of a row (`cash_leg=none|reward|cash`, `refunds=`), written and read back, and `_refuse_unwritable_cash`, `_row_named`: `json_to_tt_lines` refuses a row whose line would move other cash, read back, than the row does (`src/taxjson/lib/cash_legs.py` — `cash_leg_of`, `cash_meaning`, `same_cash_meaning`, `_is_reward`, `_reward_cash`; the kinds are `src/taxjson/lib/row_types.py` — `CASH_LEG_KINDS`; the row field `src/taxjson/model/transaction.py` — `cash_leg`; the refunds' token `src/taxjson/model/evidence.py` — `commission_refunds_tt`, `commission_refunds_of_tt`; backlog TJ-5036); `SETTLED_KEY`, `TRADED_KEY`, `date_token`, `_take_line_keys`, `_check_traded`, `_check_date_gap`, `MAX_DATE_GAP_DAYS`: a row's other date when its line's date is not both (`settled=`, read into `date_settle` by `_check_corp`; `traded=`, read into `date`; backlog TJ-5211, TJ-5213); `NOT_TT_KEY`, `NOT_TT_WHY`, `not_tt_why`, `_unwritable_cash`: the `not_tt=` marker the events view ends a line with for a row the writer refuses, which the reader refuses if pasted (backlog TJ-5212); `FUTURES_SETTLEMENT_TOKEN`, `SETTLE_KEY`, `_check_futures_settlement`: a settled futures part's facts (backlog TJ-0662); `_tt_contract_size`: the contract size a line's total is checked at, ticker.map `MULT` first (backlog TJ-0864); `_check_total_sign`, `_check_futures_total`, `_refuse_negative_price`, `_refuse_futures_assign`, `_excess_commission_sale`: the sign of a trade line's total (a negative sale total only where the line's fee explains it, at a price of 0 or above), a futures BUYSELL line's read against its own price (either sign of a buy's cost above 0; at a negative price the size and qty x price x size +/- fee with its sign, anything else refused), a negative price on anything but a plain future refused at its line, and a future's ASSIGN line refused at its line with the settlement's reason (only BUYSELL fills are booked); `_check_futures_settlement` refuses a settled buying leg an older convert-tt wrote with negative money at a price above 0 (backlog TJ-5162).
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_account`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/inputs.py` — `_refuse_crypto_openings`: where an account's `.tt` files are converted; an OPENING line in a crypto account is refused.
- `src/taxjson/lib/check_tt.py` — `check_project`, `check_paths`, `check_file`, `parse_line`, `render`, `Problem`, `FileReport`, `_taxable_transfers`, `_opening_snapshots` (one snapshot date per symbol per account, `lib/opening.snapshots`), `_as_in_project`, `project_root`; `src/taxjson/cli/explain.py` — `cmd_check_tt`, `_tt_path_item`, `_tt_file`; `src/taxjson/cli/main.py` — `_check_tt_files_only`: `tjs check-tt`, every `.tt` line through the run's own readers (the converter's parsers, then read_declarations and read_tt_openings across lines, and a taxable account's TRANSFER rows through the run's netting), every problem named with its file and line, errors (refused by the run) apart from warnings (booked as written); a file named by its path needs no project, and one the run reads for its account gets the account's checks; a relative FILE is found from the shell's folder, the project's, or its inputs folder.
- `src/taxjson/lib/tt_totals.py` — `read_diag`, `project_mismatches`, `Mismatch`, `source_line` (an ACQUIRED line quoted as written): a `.tt` line whose total is not qty x price +/- fee (booked as written; the margin is `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `tolerance`), read back from the stage's .diag; `src/taxjson/pipeline/diagnostics.py` — `_echo_tt_totals` shows each on the console on every run and `--strict` stops on it; `src/taxjson/lib/checklist.py` — `d_run_clean` lists them.
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/diagnostics.py` — `_echo_tt_bare_symbols`; `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `_warn_bare_equity_symbol`, `_equity_account`: a `.tt` symbol without its market suffix on an equity account (the run passes `--equity`), shown on the console with file:line when the broker rows hold the suffixed listing.

- `tests/parser_conformance.py` — `ParserConformance`, `FIXTURES`, `parse_fixture`, `schema_findings`: the harness every parser test subclasses (schema, golden output, determinism, row accounting, detection through `tjs run`'s routing, the countries).
- `tests/test_parser_conformance.py` — `TestQuestradeConformance`, `TestIbConformance`, `TestKrakenConformance`, `TestKrakenLedgersConformance`, `UPDATE_GOLDEN`: one registration per parser (Kraken's two exports, two).
- `tests/fixtures/ib/expected.json` — `action`, `date_settle`: an example golden; each broker folder under tests/fixtures has a synthetic `sample.csv` and its `expected.json`.

## Merge, sort, dedup and validate

An account's parsed files are merged, sorted and deduplicated across files,
ticker.map is applied, amounts are converted to the base currency, fund
distributions are added, and the result is validated: the account's
`work/<name>_base.json`. `taxjson-merge2` does these steps in one call.

- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_merge2.py` — `main`, `_parse_args`, `_sort_dedup`, `_convert_stage`, `_validate_stage`, `cancel_trade_pairs`, `reconcile_dividend_tax`, `canonicalize_split_ratios`, `warn_duplicate_splits`, `_hold_openings`, `_release_openings`: the combined merge tool (`main` runs the stages; the command line, the sort and dedup, the currency conversion and the validation are its helpers). With `--incomplete-history` the merged accounts' `.tt` `OPENING ... cost=unknown` lines are position rows while the dated renames are booked and the opening cut-off is applied, and are taken out before the books are written (`src/taxjson/lib/missing_history.py` — `hold_declared_openings`, `release_declared_openings`, `replaced_opening_messages`; the crypto map stage holds them the same way: `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_ticker_map.py` — `hold_declared_openings`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_held_openings_arg`; `src/taxjson/lib/renames.py` — `_holders`, `_unknown_openings`, `_unknown_at_renames`; TJ-5007, TJ-5042). A replaced line answers for nothing downstream: `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_opening_pairs` (no transfer-in arrival); `src/taxjson/lib/first_run.py` — `replaced_openings`, `_gains_logs` (no later short sale); `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_missing_history.py` — `_mh_replaced`, `_replaced_log` (listed under REPLACED); `src/taxjson/lib/checklist.py` — `d_missing_history`.
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_merge.py` — `main`: concatenates transaction files (`--opening-cutoff`: the blended pass cuts every account's books at its OPENING snapshot again, with every account's renames; backlog TJ-0964).
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_sort.py` — `plan_dedup`, `DedupPlan`, `deduplicate`, `sort_transactions`, `_tt_near_duplicates`, `generate_uid` (a row with no id or a blank one: its content id, backlog TJ-5041), `rename_conflicts`, `RenameDestinationConflict` (two SPLIT rows renaming one holding on one day to different symbols: refused by `deduplicate` before any row is dropped, both named; backlog TJ-4985): sorting and cross-file dedup; ambiguous duplicates are reported, not dropped; two generic mappings naming different brokers are never copies (`source_brokers_of`, backlog TJ-0975); a dropped copy's income facts fill the kept row's (`_fill_income_facts`, backlog TJ-0980).
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_validate.py` — `validate_transactions`, `main`: the final shape check on a book.
- `src/taxjson/lib/json_input.py` — `read_json_doc`, `load_json_doc_or_exit`, `check_row_types`, `require_gains_doc`, `check_gains_rows`, `gains_row_kind`, `read_rows_doc` (a document a reader takes beside its own: only the rows and fields its views read are checked), `read_work_doc` (`check_gains_parts`: a gains file with none of its transactions, inventory and summary is damaged, never an empty book), `rows_or_exit`, `filing_json_text`, `dump_filing_json`: how every tool reads a JSON input (a missing list, a wrong type or a NaN / Infinity is refused; every disposition of a gains file carries its units, gain and a date — the one row contract of the filing and summary readers), and how filing figures are written (a non-finite number refused).
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_diff.py` — `main`, `_diff_read`, `_diff_pair`, `extract_records`, `make_key`, `field_diffs`: compares two transaction or gains files (ADDED / REMOVED / MODIFIED); `_diff_read` reads and checks both files, `_diff_pair` pairs their records by match key.

## ticker.map, renames, cross-listings and symbol codes

ticker.map is one keyword-prefixed rules file at the project root holding
standing truths: spellings, consolidations of two listings (TOBASE),
deletions, EXTRACT fixes, T1135 countries. A ticker change and a journal
between two listings are dated events, `.tt` lines of an account written date
first (`RENAME <date> OLD NEW`, `JOURNAL <date> FROM TO <qty>`): the position
and its cost carry to the new ticker on that date; the journal's legs join the
two listings as one security. The legacy ticker.map JOURNAL (read as TOBASE)
and dated RENAME lines still work; `tjs format-map --write` migrates
them. Two listings joined by a transfer journal become one security, and
Questrade internal codes are resolved to real tickers from the other accounts'
evidence.

- `src/taxjson/lib/ticker_map.py` — `find_ticker_map`, `read_side_rules`, `parse_side_line`, `SideRules`, `map_ticker`, `refuse_legacy_map_file`, `extract_symbol`: reading ticker.map and mapping one symbol (an EXTRACT target spelled as the books spell it, upper case and a Canadian listing ROOT.TO, a `.V` outside CAD refused).
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_ticker_map.py` — `apply_mapping`, `guard_option_listing_collisions`; `src/taxjson/lib/map_file.py` — `load_map_file`, `map_file_problems`, `_parse_map_file`, `_PARSED`, `_own_copy` (a map file parsed once a process while its text and its tobase.map overlay are unchanged, each caller given its own copy); `src/taxjson/lib/ticker_map_rules.py` — `merge_renames`, `bare_target_warnings`, `named_symbols`, `_parse_map_text`, `distinct_spellings`, `listing_spelling_notes`, `map_symbol`, `_MAP_OPTION_RE`: the `taxjson-ticker-map` stage applied to a book (an option follows its underlying's rule, an option on a future in each prefix spelling, `F:`, `/` or `\`: backlog TJ-5037); a bare US ticker beside a share listing also read as the US listing in a DISTINCT line (a rename-type line so written warned when the books hold the US listing: `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_say_listing_spellings`); the symbols a map's lines name (`lookups=True`: a lookup line's symbol too); a rename-type line joining an option or a future with shares, or two contracts, refused (`src/taxjson/lib/derivative_checks.py` — `_join_derivative_check`). A map with problems (`src/taxjson/lib/map_file.py` — `map_file_problems`) stops `tjs run` and the planning commands (buy-check, sell-check, rebuy, wash-radar, harvest) alike: `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_refuse_map_problems`; `src/taxjson/cli/plan.py` — `_strict_ticker_map`, `_wash_class_context` (`load_map_file` alone skips such a line with a warning; `src/taxjson/lib/similar.py` — `map_problems`).
- `src/taxjson/lib/rename_decl.py` — `DatedRename`, `KIND_CRYPTO` (a declaration, and the kind of account it applies to); `src/taxjson/lib/rename_rows.py` — `rename_events`, `row_source`, `row_source_id`, `SOURCE_LABELS`, `underlying_change` (the books' rename rows: their events and where each came from; an option contract's row as its underlying's change); `src/taxjson/lib/renames.py` — `apply_dated_renames`, `_book_option_series`, `_option_holders`, `_fold_late_rows`, `_booked_as_new`, `after_rename_row`, `RenameNeedsCountry`, `late_rows`, `declared_late`, `unresolved_late`, `unused_declarations`, `rename_hints`, `report`, `render`, `_walk_states`, `_walk_fifo`: renames as dated events and `tjs renames` (each rename's source; the position and book cost each rename carried, replayed by the country's lot rule, `country.lot_rule`; applied in date order, to the accounts of the declaring kind, `late=` per account — `DatedRename.late_for`, the event's own only for the accounts that held OLD; `late=fold` re-books the rows the country's engine takes after the account's own rename row — `after_rename_row` compares `event_sort_key` keys, `ca_main` or `us_main`; OLD includes the raw spellings an undated rename maps onto it; an account holding an option on OLD still open at the date carries the event, each contract still open at its rename row moves to the same contract on NEW, and while units the event moved are left in the account every later row under that contract's OLD symbol is booked as NEW whatever `late=` says (listed by `tjs renames` from the run's notes, `run_notes_missing` when there are none); a declaration that books nothing listed; the look-alike renames the exports' .diag hints show, suggested with the `.tt` line that books each).
- `src/taxjson/lib/dated_events.py` — `read_declarations`, `check_against_map`, `resolve_renames`, `project_renames`, `settle_journals`, `write_sidecars`, `journal_legs`, `effective_lines`, `tt_renames`, `rename_records`, `state_doc`, `read_state`, `legacy_note`, `home_accounts`, `account_kind`, `Journal`, `STATE`; `src/taxjson/lib/rename_migration.py` — `plan_migration`; `src/taxjson/lib/rename_decl.py` — `apply_meta`: the `.tt` JOURNAL and RENAME lines of every account (checked up front), a journal settled against the broker's own legs and booked as transfer evidence (work/<acct>_tt-journal_transfers.json), every declaration of a ticker change merged into one event (contradictions refused) and written into the run's effective map, `format-map`'s simulation of the run before and after a migration, and the record of every dated event (work/dated_events.state, each with its source and place).
- `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `parse_journal_line`, `parse_rename_line`, `_EVENT_ACTIONS`: the two dated-event `.tt` lines (date first, never rows of the converted file).
- `src/taxjson/lib/journals.py` — `report`, `render`, `source_of`, `SOURCES`, `_Map`, `_broker_journals`, `_classify`, `FORMAT`: `tjs journals` — every broker journal between two listings, per account, with how it was found and its state (joined with the map line that pools it, suggested with the lines that settle it, refused with the undo), read from work/cross_listings.state, the parsed exports and the effective map.
- `src/taxjson/lib/cross_listings.py` — `gather`, `analyze`, `shown_apart` (the exports' evidence that two same-root listings are NOT one security: a receipt, different companies), `map_lines`, `effective_map_text`, `joined_note`, `companies_differ`, `collisions`, `extract_words`, `collision_note`, `journal_wording`, `_explicit_journal`, `_journal_names_verdict`, `_trailing_form`, `_hub_partners_agree`, `business_days`, `read_state`, `state_problem` (why an existing state file reads as empty, or loses a record: its JSON, its format, each record list and record, `_records_problem`; `tjs journals` and the checklist refuse it), `STATE_KEYS`: two listings joined by their transfer journal (the pairs left alone — a DISTINCT line, the user's map, two companies — recorded as `refused` for `tjs journals`) (a broker's explicit journal pair by its legs' own names; the window in business days); a symbol naming two companies (a fund's US-dollar unit beside an NYSE stock) and the EXTRACT line that separates them; a Questrade currency journal the parser paired, joined as a TOBASE line (Canada only); a `.tt` JOURNAL line's pair joined when one root or agreeing names show one security (`declared`, `_claim`, `declared_verdict`, `listing_root` — the venue suffix only —, `receipt_why` — a depositary receipt is its own root —, `declared_twins`, `partial_overlaps`, `near_restatements`; both countries; else refused `UNPROVEN` and the run stops), its record carrying `source`; a pair the user's map decides read through the map's renames (`analyze` `map_renames`: an out-leg the map books as the in-leg's other listing joins the in-leg to it; legs the map books as two symbols are a Warning, `map_split`, `map_split_note`); a broker-referenced journal's legs pair inside their account before any cross-account cancelling (`analyze` step 0b; a reference group is one journal by `ref_group_journal`, the rule `src/taxjson/lib/missing_history.py` — `_detected_journals` shares with `src/taxjson/lib/transfer_in.py` — `own_journal_legs`).
- `src/taxjson/lib/symbol_codes.py` — `resolve`, `project_evidence`, `names_agree`, `is_code`, `read_state`, `codes_note`, `exact_name`, `questrade_name`, `questrade_own_name`, `code_designators`, `listing_designators`, `designators_agree`, `rbc_name`, `_CONFIRM_RE`, `_cut_account_ref`: Questrade internal codes resolved to tickers (the account's own rows first, `how` "account"; a code's warrant / right / unit / preferred / class designators read over all its descriptions must agree with the listing's); the security names (dealer confirmation wording and a transfer's account reference cut) that codes and cross-listings compare. `src/taxjson/lib/corp_actions.py` — `parse_questrade_corporate_actions` (`symbol_codes`) and `src/taxjson/lib/corp_extract.py` — `extract_events` (`--symbol-codes`) book a spinoff chain under an internal code as the record resolves it (the event id keeps the code). The Questrade parser's description key (a dividend row matched to its trades) is here too: `src/taxjson/lib/symbol_codes.py` — `_get_desc_key` (`questrade_name` builds on it).
- `src/taxjson/lib/listing_suffix.py` — `scan_questrade`, `scan_rbc`, `project_evidence`, `resolve`, `fixes`, `corrections_note`, `suggestions`: a Questrade / RBC bare ticker's listing read from the books, not the row currency, a transfer's out-leg read through the map's renames (applied by `taxjson-brokerage --listing-fixes`; another account's proof for the same broker and name counts, `Evidence.proved`); a Questrade dividend reinvestment binds to the account's held listing (`src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/questrade.py` — `_rei_listing`).

## Corporate actions and elections

Mergers, spin-offs, tenders and reorganizations are read from each broker's
corporate-action rows into `CorporateAction` events. Each event needs an
election (taxable or a rollover, by country); the choice is saved in a
manifest and turned into transaction rows. Undecided events stop the run with
an election list (a sheltered account's spin-off or merger is booked without
asking unless `sheltered_elections = "ask"`); `tjs elect` records the
choices. Splits and renames use
one shared timeline so their arithmetic is the same everywhere.

- `src/taxjson/lib/corp_actions.py` — `CorporateAction`, `parse_ib_corporate_actions`, `parse_questrade_corporate_actions`, `RULES_BY_COUNTRY`, `RuleSpec`, `resolve_event`, `options_for`, `apply_auto_defaults`, `IGNORE_ELECTION`, `Manifest`, `ElectionRecord`, `_ib_union` (an IB account's statements' rows read together, a row repeated with another value kept once), `combine_broker_copies` (`_same_listing`, `_value_differences`, `_larger_copy`: which copies are one holding, what they disagree on, which is kept): the broker extractors; the election rules per country; the saved elections (the manifest) and dedup of one event seen by two brokers. RBC Direct's extractor reads the RBC parser's rows, so it sits above both: `src/taxjson/lib/rbc_corp_actions.py` — `parse_rbc_corporate_actions`, `_rbc_ca_symbol`, `_rbc_context_rows`, `_rbc_row_homes`.
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_corp_actions.py` — `main`, `EXTRACTORS` (by parser id), `extractor_for` (a label folded through the registry), `_prompt_election`, `_pending_doc`, `_emit_resolved`, `EXIT_ELECTIONS_REQUIRED`: the `taxjson-corp-actions` stage (`--sheltered-elections`: a sheltered account's events booked without asking); an extractor run over a broker group's files, `src/taxjson/lib/corp_extract.py` — `extract_events`.
- `src/taxjson/lib/corp_actions.py` — `_allocated_spinoff_selling_fraction`, `CIL_TAKEOVER_TYPE`, `drop_broker_cil_pairs`, `warn_unsold_spinoff_cil` (`read_tt_sales`, `_tt_fraction_sale`: a fraction sold by hand in the account's `.tt` files, passed by the run with `--tt`), `_qt_spinoff_cil`, `rbc_spinoff_cil_fraction`, `rbc_cil_dated`, `cil_paid_days` (a payment made again after a reversal keeps the reversed payment's day), `dated_cil_parts`, `_cil_sale_parts`, `_fraction_sales`, `_own_cil_parts`: an s.86.1 spin-off's cash in lieu of a fraction sold at the cash (CA-CORP-05): the Questrade and RBC extractors attach the CIL row to the spin-off event, the rollover books the sale (one sale per day the cash was paid, `cash_in_lieu_parts`: two broker accounts of one account, or an RBC account's rows of two days), and `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_merge2.py` — `drop_broker_cil_pairs` drops Questrade's own $0 pair for it.
- `src/taxjson/lib/corp_actions.py` — `SHELTERED_DEFAULT`, `sheltered_elections_mode`, `sheltered_default_applies`, `sheltered_default_rows`, `sheltered_default_text`: a sheltered account's spin-off ($0 cost) or merger (cost carried) booked without asking, `[settings] sheltered_elections`.
- `src/taxjson/cli/elect.py` — `cmd_elect` (its parts: `_elect_pending_view`, `_elect_all_view`, `_elect_one_set`, `_elect_set_event`, `_elect_set_save`, `_elect_saved`, `_elect_clear`, `_elect_redo`), `_print_pending`, `_print_elections`, `_elections_json_out` (every `--json` listing, one serializer); `src/taxjson/pipeline/inputs.py` — `_warn_zero_value_spinoffs`, `_note_sheltered_defaults`, `_sheltered_elections`, `_country_has_corp_rules`: `tjs elect` and the run's election messages.
- `src/taxjson/lib/corporate_timeline.py` — `SplitTimeline` (`created_on`: whether a SPLIT placed on a day created a symbol, on the timeline's date basis; `class_at`: the dated identity classes, one per rename epoch; `identical`, `_chain`: the Canadian denial pass's row-by-row test within a class, a rename target's own rows before the split window of the rename being another security, backlog TJ-2699), `cumulative_factor`, `split_event_key`, `split_seen`, `split_copies` (the one duplicate-split rule every walk of split events uses, dict rows and engine rows alike), `event_sort_key`, `renames_at_open` (US engine: a rename its target trades ahead of), `account_tie`, `radar_priority`: split and rename arithmetic and same-day event order (rows of two accounts at one moment in account-name order, CA-DATE-14 / US-DATE-13; the engines also take their input books in that order, `src/taxjson/engine/rows.py` — `_by_account_name`).
- `src/taxjson/lib/corp_views.py` — `spinoffs`, `splits`, `render_spinoffs`, `render_splits`, `wrong_country_elections`, `_held`, `_rows`: `tjs spinoffs` and `tjs splits` (`_rows`: the books through `json_input.read_work_doc`, a damaged one refused); `_held` replays the shares held through `balance_on` (`src/taxjson/lib/unit_balance.py`), so a split is applied at the open like the balance walk does; a saved `fmv_per_share=0` beside a broker-reported value is explained (the broker's value is booked), never flagged; `_cil_booked` finds a consolidation fraction's booked cash-in-lieu sale (within `CIL_DAYS`), so `splits` notes it `CIL`, not `FRACTION`. `spinoffs` takes `extractor_for` from its caller (`src/taxjson/cli/explain.py` — `cmd_spinoffs`, from bin/taxjson_corp_actions): lib reaches neither bin nor the parser registry (`_current_events`).

## Currency conversion and FX rates

Every amount is converted to the home currency (CAD or USD) at the day's
rate. `stage_currency_rates` builds the project's rates file by calling
`taxjson-to-base-curr` per source currency (Bank of Canada, with fallbacks);
the rates are cached in the home folder.

- `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_currency_rates`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/inputs.py` — `_rates_coverage_stale`, `_raw_align_adjust_currency`, `_raw_net_withholding`, `_home_currency`: the run's rate file, its freshness, and ADJUST rows (and a purchase whose `listing_currency` is not its cash's, a Questrade REI) restated in the pool's currency for the raw holdings books, and a dividend's withholding paid in another currency netted at its own day's rate.
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_rates_inputs`, `RATES_START_STAMP`; `src/taxjson/lib/rates_window.py` — `window_start`, `earliest_in_text`, `PAD_DAYS`: the first date the rates must cover, from the earliest date written in the project's exports, `.tt` files, slips and positions snapshots (never later than January 1 of the year), recorded beside the rates file.
- `src/taxjson/bin/to_base_curr.py` — `build_rates`, `fetch_boc`, `fetch_boc_noon`, `fetch_yahoo`, `refresh_boc`; `src/taxjson/lib/rate_cache.py` — `resolve_rows`, `CACHE_FILE`: daily rates for one currency pair.
- `src/taxjson/lib/read_through.py` — `refuse_copies`: bin/to_base_curr and bin/fill_crypto_prices refuse a copy of a cache name they read through lib/rate_cache and lib/crypto_prices (the cache path, the loaders, the lookups a test fakes): setting one there raises, naming the module that holds it (`tests/test_cache_paths.py`).
- `src/taxjson/bin/to_base_curr.py` — `_drop_bad_shapes`, `_drop_bad_obs`; `src/taxjson/lib/rate_cache.py` — `_is_date`: the rate cache's damaged records (a bad shape, a rate that is not a positive number, a day that is not a real date) dropped and said before any date arithmetic.
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_convert_currency.py` — `main`, `convert_transaction`, `get_rate_for_date`, `MissingRateError`, `abort_if_currency_uncovered`, `fallback_rows`, `emit_fallback_summary`, `rate_source_summary`, `default_rate_for`: converting a book (a relabelled row keeps its `trade_currency`, the wash radar's settlement calendar); rows priced with a fallback rate, and the summary of rate sources. The rates file: `src/taxjson/lib/exchange_rates.py` — `load_exchange_rates`, `norm_currency`, `RateHistory`, `parse_rate_line`, `RateLine`, `is_calendar_date`, `bad_date_refusal`, `RatesFileError` (a refused row: one `Error:` line and exit 2 everywhere) (the one grammar of a rates-file line, which lib/base_rates reads its lines with too: a real calendar date, a plain positive finite decimal).
- `src/taxjson/lib/json_cache.py` — `save_json_cache`: atomic, locked saves of the shared price and rate caches (`merge`: entries on disk kept; `update`: a read-modify-write under the lock).
- `src/taxjson/bin/to_base_curr.py` — `save_cache`, `_cache_delta`, `_apply_delta`; `src/taxjson/lib/rate_cache.py` — `load_cache`, `LoadedCache`: the rate cache's save writes this run's changes (set, dropped, coverage added or removed) into the file as it is now, so concurrent runs keep each other's series.
- `src/taxjson/bin/to_base_curr.py` — `_coverage_op`, `_series_obs`: a day's coverage goes with its rate in that save (a cleanup uncovers a damaged day only while the file still holds the damage; a day asked for again is covered again), so a concurrent repair stays usable offline.
- `src/taxjson/lib/offline.py` — `offline_enabled`, `ENV_VAR`: `TAXJSON_OFFLINE`, the switch that forbids network egress.
- `src/taxjson/engine/rows.py` — `convert_currency`: the engine-side currency helper.

## Fund distributions and income dating

Non-cash fund distributions (reinvested capital gains, return of capital) are
entered in taxjson.toml `[[distributions]]` and added to the books. Which
tax year an income row belongs to, and what counts as a payment in lieu, is
decided per country from neutral facts the parsers record.

- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_apply_distributions.py` — `apply_distributions`, `main`, `resolve_live_symbol`, `_warn_roc_overlaps`, `_snapshot_after`, `_snapshot_on`: the `taxjson-apply-distributions` stage (a `--ticker-map` with problems stops it before it writes, exit 2, `_say_map_problems` naming the map and the tobase.map each problem is in: backlog TJ-5044); `_stamp_date`, `_stamp_problem`: the ADJUST's trade date in a trade-date-ordered engine (backdated before a sale straddling the record date; the row's `record_date` lets the US engine's `dist` ADJUST branch in `src/taxjson/engine/us/walk.py` take the lots settled by it only). The units held on the record date: `src/taxjson/lib/unit_balance.py` — `balance_on`.
- `src/taxjson/lib/project_tables.py` — `distribution_rows`, `claimed_losses`, `DIST_TABLE`, `CGD_TABLE`; `src/taxjson/lib/config_check.py` — `table_problems`: the hand-entered year tables in taxjson.toml.
- `src/taxjson/lib/cg_dividends.py` — `parse_map`, `entries_from_config`, `allocate`, `CgDividendMapError`: T5 box 18 capital-gains dividends.
- `src/taxjson/characterize/income_dating.py` — `IncomeRules`, `parse_ric_entries`, `is_canadian_issuer`, `split_share_roots`: income-year and payment-in-lieu rules (PLAN step 14 moved them from lib, a module of its own beside characterize_rows: work/ keeps the pay dates); `is_return_of_capital`: which ADJUST rows are a return of capital (slip-audit's T3 box 42 and the roc-sum total). `src/taxjson/lib/income_dating.py` — `rules_for`, `from_settings`: the rules built from a project's [settings] (characterize does not read project.settings); its re-exports of the other names went after v1.0.0. `src/taxjson/parsers/toolbox/descriptions.py` — `PIL_LABEL_RETURN_OF_CAPITAL`: the label a parser puts on a payment in lieu of a return of capital.
- `src/taxjson/lib/pipeline.py` — `apply_roc_record_dates`, `apply_trust_roc_record_dates`, `income_dating_flags`, `add_income_dating_args`: how the gains run applies them.

## Transfers, crypto sends and opening balances

TRANSFER rows are custody evidence, kept out of the books unless something
decides otherwise. Shares that arrive in a taxable account from outside the
books are booked at the broker's stated book value, or flagged. In a US
project a move between two of your own taxable accounts carries its lots. A
crypto send that never arrives in another of your accounts may be a
disposition, decided once and saved. Opening balances come from a broker's
positions report.

- `src/taxjson/lib/transfer_in.py` — `Arrival`, `arrivals`, `OWN_MOVE_DAYS`, `SLOW_MOVE_DAYS`, `slow_pairs`, `SlowMove`, `slow_dates`, `LISTING_JOURNAL_DAYS`, `sidecar_rows`, `own_journal_legs`, `movable_rows`, `stated_book_value`, `booked_rows`, `mark_covered`, `_tt_events`, `_tt_units_at`, `_MovedOut` (the units a move of your own took out of an account that no broker row accounts for: they spend the `.tt` purchase unless units arrive within `SLOW_MOVE_DAYS` after it), `attention_lines`: shares that arrived by transfer; a journal inside one account settled within its own pair; an out leg cancels an in leg of the same security within 10 days, a listing journal two listings of one root, then a slower move of your own (`slow_pairs`: the in leg 11 to 45 days after an out leg of the same quantity in another account, neither with another candidate; `Arrival.departures` the earlier out legs nothing paired, named on the ATTENTION line; one pairing the arrival booking, Canada's unit legs and the US lot carry all read, `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `own_move_plan`, `_paired_transfers`, `_custody_pairing` (`OwnMove.slow`, `out_left`, `in_left`: a slower move, a pairing that takes part of a row; backlog TJ-0269); an in-kind move's own leg, booked or not, never pairs so (`no_move`, backlog TJ-0230); the purchase drafts read the same pairing, `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_missing_history.py` — `_write_purchases` (`own_move_plan`, given to `src/taxjson/lib/purchase_drafts.py` — `draft_purchases` as `own_moves`, `_draft_transfers`; backlog TJ-0599)); the .tt coverage replay (splits, sales before the arrival, partial coverage said and not booked).
- `src/taxjson/lib/in_kind.py` — `Leg`, `Move`, `legs`, `pair_all`, `Pairing`, `pair`, `_parts`, `line_legs`, `in_parts`, `parts_message`, `apply_lines`, `decide`, `contract_size`, `value`, `booked_rows`, `mark_sheltered`, `message`, `contribution_results`: in-kind contributions and withdrawals between a taxable and a registered account (CA-INKIND-* / US-INKIND-*); `_parts` finds a declared move's legs delivered in parts (every candidate counts towards uniqueness, within `_PARTS_STEPS`), `contract_size` the option contract size a move is valued and priced at (the row's `multiplier`, else lib/core's rule).
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `in_kind_state`, `stage_in_kind_context`, `_say_in_kind`, `in_kind_taxable_legs`, `in_kind_leg_idents`, `_registered_transfer_rows`, `_inkind_lines`, `_in_kind_close`; `src/taxjson/lib/in_kind.py` — `in_kind_unbooked`; `src/taxjson/cli/estimate.py` — `in_kind_fix_line`: the run's in-kind moves (work/in_kind.json), the plan's purchase marked in work/sheltered_base.json, the one warning; the moves NOT booked, read back by `sum` and the checklist's transfers step.
- `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `parse_inkind_line`: the `.tt` INKIND line (a value, never a row of the books).
- `src/taxjson/lib/transfer_in.py` — `NotOwn`, `read_not_own`, `not_own_text`, `not_own_account_problem`, `leg_ident`; `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `parse_not_own_line`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `own_move_plan`: the `.tt` NOTOWN line, which undoes one own-move pairing (`arrivals`' exact or slow pair; the US lot carry reads the same pairing, so it leaves the legs alone) and never makes one (backlog TJ-0075).
- `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `stage_transfer_arrivals`, `transfer_arrivals`, `own_move_plan`, `_paired_transfers`, `_transfer_key` (`effective=True`: the arrivals key a row through the map the books were merged with, work/ticker.map.effective with the run's journal joins), `stage_own_account_moves`, `own_account_custody_moves`, `_stage_crypto_sends`; `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `cmd_crypto_sends` (its `--set` decisions, each checked and saved: `_crypto_sends_set`), `_print_crypto_sends` (the listing; each send's facts: `_crypto_sends_facts`; the stablecoin gifts' and payments' currency gain by year: `_crypto_sends_fx_gain`; the USD/stablecoin pool's notes: `_crypto_sends_pool_notes`), `cmd_opening` (the day checked: `_opening_snapshot`; one broker account: `_opening_broker_accounts`; the file and an earlier opening of its symbols: `_opening_target`), `_opening_lines`, `_opening_iso_day`, `_opening_padded_day`: where the run books arrivals and own-account moves; the crypto sends hook and `tjs crypto-sends`; `tjs opening`, which writes OPENING lines from a positions report (its date checked with the `.tt` reader's YYYY-MM-DD rule, each line read back before the file is written; an existing file replaced with `--force` as a user's file, `src/taxjson/lib/safe_write.py` — `write_user_file`).
- `src/taxjson/lib/transfer_in.py` — `OwnMove`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_stage_pooled_own_moves`, `_move_legs`; `src/taxjson/engine/ca/own_moves.py` — `_own_move_view`, `_own_move_units`; `src/taxjson/lib/row_types.py` — `LOT_MOVE_TYPE`; `src/taxjson/lib/corporate_timeline.py` — `_walk_rest`: Canada's moves of your own between two taxable accounts (CA-ACB-OWN-MOVE, backlog TJ-0093): the transfer-in pairing names the units it cancelled between two accounts, the run's transfer-arrivals stage writes them as lot-move legs to work/<account>_own_moves.json (coins: the crypto-sends pairing's, written by the own-account moves stage, backlog TJ-0281), and the Canadian engine moves the units in one account's own books only (the blended pass drops the legs: one ACB pool); the history walks read an in leg at the start of its day. A sale there of units whose unknown cost an own move alone brought names the sender (`src/taxjson/engine/ca/walk.py` — `own_move_from`; `src/taxjson/engine/ca/pool.py` — `taint`; `src/taxjson/engine/ca/own_moves.py` — `_own_move_only`; the US engine's moved lot its `moved_from`), and one drawing on an OPENING cost=unknown row names that row (`src/taxjson/engine/journal.py` — `_opening_source`; `src/taxjson/engine/ca/pool.py` — `add_sources`; `src/taxjson/engine/ca/own_moves.py` — `_unknown_sources`; `src/taxjson/engine/ca/walk.py` — `unknown_cost_from`: the pool's `unknown_from`, carried by the pool's taint_from (engine/ca/pool.py) through renames and journals without re-marking the taint's cause (TJ-3235) and by the pending ledger's `sources` to an option's shares; the US lots' `unknown_from`; backlog TJ-3209), and the account's `.sum` says their cost is in the blended figures, not a purchase missing (`src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_form_export.py` — `manual_section`, `own_move_section`; backlog TJ-0279).
- `src/taxjson/lib/crypto_sends.py` — `load_transfer_rows`, `match_transfers`, `_assign` (the send/arrival pairing: a lexicographic assignment — most pairs, fewest coins lost, closest in time), `build_report`, `record_decision`, `clear_decision`, `_manifest_lock` (a decision's load, change and save under one lock), `prompt_undecided`, `render_tt`, `DECISIONS`, `account_pool_flows`, `merge_pool_flows`, `kraken_account_flows` (the stablecoin pool's flows: each account's overlapping exports read once, a Kraken account's ledger rows joined before their refids are grouped, the accounts added up), `stale_evidence` (the transfer evidence older than the exports, or parsed from others): crypto sends, their decisions and the generated crypto_sends.tt.
- `src/taxjson/lib/crypto_sends.py` — `fair_value`, `tt_line`, `keep_written_lines`: a send's value at the full-precision price; a line written before kept.
- `src/taxjson/lib/base_rates.py` — `load_rates`, `Rates`, `RateTable`: the project's base-currency rates (work/to_base.csv) the crypto readers price with, each line read by `src/taxjson/lib/exchange_rates.py` — `parse_rate_line`.
- `src/taxjson/lib/opening.py` — `opening_row` (the row an opening position is booked as, shared by the .tt converter's OPENING line and the generic mapping's `opening` target), `OPENING_TIME`, `apply_opening_cutoff`, `snapshots`, `OpeningError`, `ATTENTION_OPENING`, `_realizations_left_out`, `_nil_lot_held`, `_refusal`: OPENING rows and the cutoff they impose on earlier rows (a left-out sale, short cover or return of capital that may go beyond the cost, of the tax year, stops the run). The share rows a snapshot replaced are named on its row (`_note_cut_legs`, `opening_cut_legs`; a row a rename made the snapshot's with its own symbol; a `.tt` `OPENING ... cost=unknown` line it replaced as a leg of action `UNKNOWN_OPENING_LEG`, which `unknown_opening_reading` reads so that no stage opens that line again: TJ-5007; a line only another account's rename ties to the snapshot is refused, `BY_OTHER_ACCOUNT`: TJ-5241; the walk back names a held line in its refusals, `_walk_note`, `_line_shown`, `_mismatch_refusal`, `_snapshot_facts`; the texts: `src/taxjson/lib/missing_history.py` — `two_readings_text`, `replaced_line_text`), and `src/taxjson/engine/premium_ledger.py` — `_in_snapshot`, `_delivery_cut`, `_renamed_into_snapshot`, `renamed_delivery_underlying`, `_say_amount_on_delivered_row` (an exercise row with an amount booked as delivered into a snapshot: said, both readings) reads there whether the snapshot's cost already holds an exercised option's premium (the walks ask it before calling an exercise delivered or cash-settled: `src/taxjson/engine/ca/walk.py` — `_assign_rolls`; `src/taxjson/engine/us/walk.py` — `_us_assign_route`) (`src/taxjson/model/evidence.py` — `cut_leg_symbol`, `CutLeg`).
- `src/taxjson/lib/pipeline.py` — `_handle_transfers`, `_net_cross_account_transfers`, `TransferValidationError`, `transfers_in_loss_windows`, `transfers_as_acquisitions`: transfer handling before the engine runs (its windows: `src/taxjson/lib/transfer_windows.py` — `_TRANSFER_PAIR_MAX_GAP_DAYS`, `_TRANSFER_SEGMENT_MAX_SPAN_DAYS`, `_TRANSFER_NEAR_TRADE_PAD_DAYS`, `_ATTEST_BLESS_PAD_DAYS`, `_RESTATEMENT_MIN_SYMBOLS`, `_JOURNAL_CANDIDATE_PAD_DAYS`).
- `src/taxjson/lib/share_exchange_windows.py` — `share_exchanges_in_loss_windows`, `share_exchange_message`, `is_share_exchange`, `SHARE_EXCHANGE_ELECTIONS`, `ATTENTION_SHARE_EXCHANGE`: a share-exchange merger booked as a cost carry-over (a SPLIT row whose `corp_election` says so; a name change's `rename` does not) whose shares arrive inside a taxable loss's 30-day window: Canada's run says each one as ATTENTION, marked for the end-of-run list, and denies nothing (`src/taxjson/lib/pipeline.py` — `run_gains`; docs/limits.md); only where the shares are still held at day 30 (`_held_at_day_30`). The pooled pass's line (the loss in one account, the exchange in another) is echoed by `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `ATTENTION_SHARE_EXCHANGE` and listed from the `<account>_blend.diag` mirror by `src/taxjson/lib/first_run.py` — `parser_warnings`.

## Missing purchase history

When the files do not reach back to a purchase, a sale has no cost. These
sales are detected, listed with suggestions, and can be answered with a
dated `.tt` line `OPENING <date> <SYMBOL> <qty> cost=unknown` (an opening
lot the gains run synthesizes; the project-root missing_history.json of
older versions is refused and `tjs migrate` converts it) or a draft of
the missing purchases.

- `src/taxjson/lib/missing_history.py` — `detect_missing_history`, `MissingHistoryCandidate`, `classify_year_shorts`, `missing_history_suspects`, `detect_zero_basis_acquisitions`, `load_missing_history`, `synthesize_openings` (two holdings renamed into one symbol are not sized: `_converging_renames`, backlog TJ-0959; a line opens its quantity on its date; a pair without one — a suggestion, an old entry being converted — is sized from the rows through the tax year's end: `sizing_until`; `short_again_message`), `stale_missing_history_entries`, `MISSING_HISTORY_FILE`, `detect_superficial_loss_warnings`, `_walk_key`, `walk_journal_symbols`, `detected_journal_symbols`, `journal_targets`, `_journal_line_symbols`, `JournalDays`, `_journal_trade_day`, `books_journal_days`, `undeclared_journal_days`, `read_tt_openings`, `TtOpening`, `TtOpeningError`, `MISSING_HISTORY_TT`, `missing_history_arg`, `project_missing_history`, `has_tt_openings`, `_apply_fixed_openings`, `_symbol_on`, `openings_from_log`, `plan_tt_write`, `apply_tt_plan`, `default_opening_date`, `read_legacy_entries`, `legacy_missing_history_file`, `MissingHistoryNoLongerRead`: finding sales with no purchase; the `.tt` OPENING cost=unknown lines (read through the project's missing-history path, applied with their date and quantity; written by find-missing-history and migrate); the project file and the openings synthesized from it; purchase drafts and loss-window warnings; the walks' order, where a journal between a security's two lines (a join of the run, a Questrade `journal_pair`, RBC's J~ reference on an RBC export, a `.tt` JOURNAL line, a legacy JOURNAL line) reads a day's buys and in-legs first on its days only (its legs' days and its own trades' day — its quantity, FROM bought and TO sold; a TOBASE line names none, and the run names the `.tt` line for a day that looks like one). The migration, `src/taxjson/lib/history_migration.py` — `plan_missing_history_migration`, `project_view`, `apply_missing_history_migration`; the purchase drafts, `src/taxjson/lib/purchase_drafts.py` — `draft_purchases`, `_lot_line_date` (an IB lot on its settlement date under tax_date settle). The journal legs it reads (a Norbert's gambit's trades, a TRANSFER row's journal key, a reference group's legs) are `src/taxjson/lib/cross_listings.py` — `_opposite_trades`, `journal_leg_key`, `ref_group_journal`.
- `src/taxjson/engine/ca/router.py` — `_MarkedShortRouter`, `_wants_short`, `note`, `both_open`, `_say_late_covers`, `_say_both_open`; `src/taxjson/lib/brokerages/base.py` — `SHORT_MARKS`; `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_brokerage.py` — `_brokerage_clean_row`; `src/taxjson/model/transaction.py` — `short_marks`; `src/taxjson/lib/missing_history.py` — `drop_answered_covers`, `_long_by_its_broker`, `detect_unbacked_covers`: which purchase closes a marked short (Canada). The reading is the broker's convention, carried on each trade row as `short_marks` (stamped from the parser's own attribute: `description` for RBC alone): there only a purchase marked `COVER SHORT.` closes the short and every other purchase is a long purchase, in any file; an IB or Questrade row, a `.tt` line or the generic importer's covers while the account holds no long. The run says a marked cover that finds the short already covered by an unmarked purchase (`marked cover`) and an account that ends long and short the same shares (`long and short`). The history check (`tjs find-missing-history`, the run's unanswered covers) reads the same covers by country: the US engine nets the first buys.
- `src/taxjson/bin/taxjson_missing_history.py` — `main` (its parts: `_mh_args`, `_mh_unchecked`, `_mh_links`, `_mh_country`, `_mh_journal`, `_mh_drop_covers`, `_mh_candidates`, `_mh_short_rows`, `_mh_covers_stale`, `_mh_zero`, `_mh_print_found`, `_mh_covered_by`, `_mh_links_section`, `_mh_truncated`, `_mh_zero_section`, `_mh_renamed`, `_mh_open_rows`, `_mh_fix_acts`, `_mh_fix_steps`), `_print_section`, `_opening_sizes`, `_print_zero_section`, `_write_purchases`, `_entries_file`, `_entry_row` (a REMOVE / STALE row names the file and line it is on), `_in_kind_withdrawals`, `_in_kind_of` (`--in-kind`: a short an in-kind withdrawal NOT booked explains is said so, its fix the move's INKIND line), `_late_of`, `_late_line` (`--late-rename`: a short a trade after a rename makes, its fix the RENAME line): the `taxjson-missing-history` tool.
- `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `cmd_find_missing_history` (its `--write-missing-history` branch: `_find_missing_history_write`), `_write_missing_history_tt`, `_late_rename_trades` (a short an undeclared trade after a rename makes: named, never written as an opening); `src/taxjson/pipeline/stages.py` — `_missing_history_arg`; `src/taxjson/cli/migrate.py` — `_migrate_missing_history`; `src/taxjson/cli/positions.py` — `_missing_history_suspects`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/run.py` — `_year_short_rows`, `_refuse_unusable_missing_history`; `src/taxjson/pipeline/config.py` — `_refuse_legacy_project_files`; `src/taxjson/cli/books.py` — `_refuse_stale_books_write` (no write flag on books older than the inputs): `tjs find-missing-history`, `tjs migrate`'s conversion and the run's use of the lines.
- `src/taxjson/lib/migrate.py` — `MISSING_HISTORY_FILES`, `legacy_files`, `legacy_message`: the old missing_history.json (and phantoms.json) refused like the old per-purpose maps.
- `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `parse_unknown_opening_line`, `unknown_opening_text`, `UNKNOWN_OPENING_FORM`: the `.tt` line's form.
- `src/taxjson/lib/option_close_check.py` — `unbacked_option_closes`, `unbacked_option_close_messages`, `_clocked` (a same-day opening backs a close only while the day's order is unknown: no clock on a row, or one stamp): option rows the broker marks closing that the books cannot back (the test: `src/taxjson/lib/unbacked_close.py` — `unbacked_close`, `OrderStarts`).

## The model: rows, records and money

The lowest layer, `src/taxjson/model/`: it imports only itself and the standard library (`tests/test_model_money.py` holds it to that), and lib and the layers above import it.

- `src/taxjson/model/transaction.py` — `TaxTransaction`, `compute_id`, `event_identity`, `to_dict`, `INCOME_FACT_FIELDS`, `EVIDENCE_FIELDS`: the row every parser emits and the engines read (money as float or int, its id a hash of their reprs, with the corporate event of a generated row, on the actions a `.tt` line says it on, `EVENT_ID_ACTIONS`, and a rename's destination, `event_identity`, which `src/taxjson/lib/tt_format.py` — `compute_tt_id` shares: backlog TJ-4985). It left `lib/core.py` in step 6a.
- `src/taxjson/model/money.py` — `Money`, `to_money`, `from_money`, `float_decimal`, `exact_decimal`, `text_decimal`, `round_half_up`, `round_floats`, `_QTY_FIELDS`, `of`, `kept`, `engine`, `leave`, `trapped`, `MoneySiteError`, `money_error`, `stopped_text`, `shown`, `_SHOWN`, `units`, `present`, `cents_apart`: money as Decimal, taken from a float's repr, so a float or an int comes back bit for bit and of its own type; the one reader of a number written out (`text_decimal`, for model/evidence and model/result); the output's half-up rounding (by key: quantities and day counts are not rounded), moved from lib/numeric in step 6c; and the engines' money (steps 6d to 6f): the engines read a money figure as `M.of(x)`, its exact Decimal (step 6f took the float mode out). `engine` wraps each engine's compute_gains (`src/taxjson/engine/ca/taxrules.py` — `CanadaTaxRules`; `src/taxjson/engine/us/taxrules.py` — `USATaxRules`): it runs in a decimal context of its own (`trapped`: a site mixing float into money stops on a TypeError, since it traps `FloatOperation` and a Decimal ordered against a float stops too; a Decimal rounds and formats half up, as the gains file writes), raised again as a `MoneySiteError` naming the site (`stopped_text`; a tool says it on one line: `src/taxjson/lib/cli_diag.py` — `guard_main`), and leaves its result's money as floats (`leave`, in place; a zero unsigned), so the code outside the engines holds floats and is never trapped; the US payments-in-lieu planner lets such a TypeError through: `src/taxjson/engine/us/taxrules.py` — `_plan_paid_compensation`). A figure stored as the row has it reads `M.kept(x)` (an int stays an int). Decimal starts at the engine's reads and ends at the gains file: the rows stay float (a TaxTransaction, a Decimal number made its float by `src/taxjson/lib/tx_load.py` — `coerce_transaction_row`; the row builders `src/taxjson/lib/corp_actions.py` and `src/taxjson/lib/futures.py` — `_part` make float rows), and the readers of what the engine wrote (`src/taxjson/lib/trace_format.py`, `src/taxjson/lib/cost_timeline.py` — `inventory_on`) read float, cut since steps 6g and 6h (the trace by `shown`, `inventory_on`'s carried figures by `units`: TJ-3127). `units` cuts a quantity or a ratio as round() does, `present` a shown figure half up; `cents_apart` is how many whole cents two figures cut apart differ by, each cut as `present` cuts it (check-filed's drift, the handoff's comparisons with the record and the checklist's form-export step pass a cent or less, so float error never trips them: step 6h(2)). A report's money is cut by `shown` (step 6h(4): half up from the operand's repr, a float back, the reports' one cut; it differs from round() only where the repr is an exact half at the cut; a float not finite and an int come back as they are, a zero keeps round()'s sign; the cut runs in a decimal context of its own, `_SHOWN`, wide enough for any float and trapping nothing, so a call inside an engine's `trapped` context neither reads nor changes it: TJ-3345, TJ-3403), a quantity or a ratio by `units` (round()'s cut). `tests/_money_oracle.py` — `figures`, `texts`, `compare`, `judge`, `books`, `suite`, `dump`, `diff`: the money A/B between two trees (a base, `--src` its `git archive`, and the branch): `dump` runs every engine golden and fuzzer book (and with `--suite` each compute_gains call of the named test modules) through one tree's engines, `diff` classes each difference noise (under half a unit of the 4th place and written the same; a per-share figure by the cost and quantity it is divided from, `DERIVED`), tie (a figure at a rounding's half: the 4th place's, or a cut the engine makes, `CUT_KEYS`), text (a printed number one unit apart), form (a zero's sign, an int or a Decimal against a float), typeerror (the new tree stopped, by its src line) or bug; a bug fails it (`--strict`: any class but same); an empty dump, a name twice or a `--suite` run that did not pass is refused. A change meant to move nothing is `same` everywhere; a flip lists its ties. What it cannot see (the gains file's 4 places, the stages after the engine, each command's cut and format) is the views A/B's: `tests/_views_ab.py` — `dump`, `diff`, `ties`, `fmt`, `compare`, `paired`, `classify`, `flip_rule`, `half_up`, `_holds`, `walk`, `writers`, `process_of`, `_pool`, `_needs`, `_match`, `_beside`, `_spelled_like`, `_realigned`, `file_rows`, `_shim_problem`, `_DIGEST_MASKS`, `_DIGEST_SORTED`, `exact_half`, `SUMMARY_VIEWS`, `Refused` (step 6h; how it explains a move, step 6h(4a): TJ-3363). `dump` runs the six project goldens through `_golden._run_project` (their views; a book that records none, the summaries that read no clock), with another tree's src (`--src`), 4-place rates (`--rates fx4`), the 4-place writers at N places (`--places`: the gains files, the cost timelines and the shim's `WIDENED`) or through a format-log copy (`--fmt-log`), every process with the shown log on, and keeps every `shown` call (by step, context, site and ordinal: its operand, places and result), the contexts that wrote each output file (`writers`: a file's opener, or the tool whose stdout went to the temp renamed into place) and every file's digest under the project after the run and each view (masked as the goldens mask, then `_DIGEST_MASKS` and `_DIGEST_SORTED`, each with its reason); a book whose run left no record of the shim or an error, a log file empty, with a line cut off or with no proc record, a process whose log was cut short (no exit record, or fewer calls than it counted), more child contexts claimed than proc records, two processes in one context, or a `--fmt-log` run with no format sink, did not run (`_shim_problem`). `diff` compares two dumps as the goldens do; `paired` pairs each of BASE's calls with NEW's of the same book, step, context, site and ordinal (a call in one tree only, another count at a site or other places is refused) and `classify` labels it same, cut (its operand the same, its result not: an exact half, NEW's result one unit further from zero) or carried (its operand moved by no more than the moves of its process's earlier cuts, each site and operand once), else unexplained. A moved number is explained only by a changed call of a process that printed that stream or wrote that file (`_pool`: the step's processes; a file's writers and their children), its BASE result as BASE printed it and its NEW result as NEW did, at the number's own places (the call's), with the same sign and BASE's commas (a JSON or TOML number by its value, at no more places than the call's: `_needs`), one number per call (`_match`, a maximum matching, book by book); a figure no changed call printed (a gains file's, which round_floats writes) is unexplained. The labels: cut, carried, bytes (the values the same, the text not, or a byte change beside the explained moves, a moved number respelled among them: `_beside`, `_spelled_like`; spacing may change only where a moved number's width realigns a column: `_realigned`) or unexplained (anything else); `--strict`: any difference fails, byte for byte (the calls need not pair), and any file whose digest differs, named: `file_rows`; and so does nothing compared. `ties` holds every call to the flip's rule (`flip_rule`: BASE's result round() of its operand, NEW's the half-up cut of its repr, `half_up`, `shown`'s own context mirrored, so an operand of 1e26 cuts there too; one that breaks it is unexplained and named) and adds the calls: each cut (with `--deep`, NEW's tree at 10 places: "same at 10 places", a file tie with the 10-place operand or "not found at 10 places"), each carried call with the cuts it could hold (`_holds`; "a tie the flip made" where NEW's operand is a half and BASE's is not), each unexplained call, a count of the changed calls behind no moved number, and with a `--fmt-log` NEW each format that printed an exact half; it fails on an unexplained or bytes difference, an unexplained call, BASE calls at a half with none changed, calls changed with nothing moved, or a format tie a half-up cut prints otherwise (money by name or not, but at a site `tests/_fmt_log.py` — `NOT_MONEY` lists as reviewed, each with its reason). `fmt` lists a `--fmt-log` dump's format ties by site and fails on those (step 6h(1b) takes them to zero). A dump with no books, a name twice or a book that did not run, two dumps of other rates, places or Python, calls that do not pair, a `--deep` dump of another src, rates or Python, not more places than NEW or missing a book, a NEW with no call in a book where BASE has some, and a `--fmt-log` dump a book of which had no format sink, are refused. The format log is `tests/_fmt_log.py` — `make`, `_Rewrite`, `_write_pyc`, `HOOK_SRC`: a copy of a tree's taxjson whose modules with a fixed-point format of a value (an f-string, `format()`, `%`, `str.format`) load from an unchecked pyc compiled from the rewritten tree (each value through a no-op that logs a print of an exact half: the site, the expression, money by `scripts/check_architecture.py` — `_money_name`, what it printed and what half up prints), the sources and line numbers as they were. The log is `tests/_shown_log/sitecustomize.py` — `TAXJSON_TEST_SHOWN_LOG`, `TAXJSON_TEST_GAINS_PLACES`, `TAXJSON_TEST_SHOWN_CTX`, `TAXJSON_TEST_SHOWN_ROOT`, `WIDENED`, `_claim`, `_call_text`, `_error`, `_install`, `_install_log`, `_install_places`, `_not_installed`, `_load_next`: every `M.shown` call, one compact line each in its process's own file (its context, site, operand, places and result: two trees' calls pair one to one), in a context the same in either tree (a step's first process "0"; a tool `run_cmd` runs, its caller's, ">", the tool and its rank; a child process, its parent's, "|", its module and its rank there: `_claim`), each tool context and each file under the project opened for writing or renamed into place, the format log's records, a "proc" record (flushed at once) and an "exit" record (its count of calls) per process it installs in (an "error" record, and a line on stderr, where taxjson is on the path but it cannot install), and the places hook (round_floats' default, the cost timeline's 4-place `round_half_up`, and `WIDENED`'s writers by their call's source, `_call_text` (its columns), a call it cannot tell an error record: the US engine's §263(h) `present` (engine/us/prepare.py) and fx_cash_v2's close-book cost, TJ-3370); the rate sets are `tests/_hermetic/__init__.py` — `RATE_SETS`, `home_env`. Tested by `tests/test_views_ab.py` — `plant_round`, `plant_flip`, `plant_idle`, `plant_timeline`, `_rows`, `_result` (the dumps' BASE a copy of the tree whose `shown` is round(), the NEW one whose `shown` is half up, so the tests read the same on either side of step 6h(4)'s flip).
- `src/taxjson/model/result.py` — `Result`, `from_json`, `to_json`, `gains_json`, `GainRow`, `Holding`, `Lot`, `WashRecord`, `Row`, `number`, `gain_before_denial` (a row's gain before any denial: the raw gain, else with no key the gain; the one rule for the traces, edge_cases, wash_radar and plan), `money_keys` (a view's money fields' JSON keys: what a reader holds to their types first), `ResultShapeError`: the gains output as a typed Result (step 6c). `from_json` copies the document down to its rows, read-only, and reads a part (`transactions`, `manual_reporting_required`, `wash_sales`, `inventory`, `lots_at`, `summary`, `by_ticker`; the rest with `get`) only when it is asked for: a null or empty part reads as empty, one of the wrong shape is refused then. A row's `quantity` is the JSON `qty`, its money a Decimal (float() of it is the float the file held). `gains_json` is how run_gains writes its output (round_floats, the cost timeline as it is, a null one dropped) and `to_json` is gains_json of a Result, so a recorded `*_gains.json` comes back byte for byte (`tests/test_model_result.py`). run_gains writes with gains_json;
