the test ids. A split (a module a class's `Formerly:` line names): the base's mover (with `scripts/extract_support.py`, a tool too) plans the split HEAD's class lines make, its refusals included, and each new file is the plan's text byte for byte (or but for its docstrings: listed); OLD gone; each file names OLD once, each class line its own class; a script's `OLD.Class` renamed; pins through a class's line, a copied mixin's in each copy; a grep stage that selected OLD selecting the files its pattern picks, the others listed as leaving it; the baseline without OLD, each file's ruff counts within the hits (ruff run on the base's OLD; the proof needs the dev venv's python3) of the lines it took, its noqa counts its comments' (the base's `noqa_in_text`); ids through the class lines (a lost or doubled duplicate named); outcomes: OLD run whole three times at the base and each file twice at HEAD, two base runs before HEAD's and one after (a clock value the base runs agree on is HEAD's too) (a process and an empty HOME each, PYTHONHASHSEED fixed, temp names seeded per test, `_SEED_SITE` and the seed in a child's environment only; Python 3.12 or later), each test and each fixture recorded with sys.monitoring (`_RECORD`): its outcome (pass, fail, error, skip and its reason, the exception's type), the test-tree lines it runs counted (a class body run inside a test too; keyed by qualname and offset, so a copy matches its original), its assertions (site and a digest per argument, a message among them), its child processes (argv, returncode, folder, a digest per variable of its environment and per line of captured output), the product lines it reaches (src/, packages/*/src, and test-tree code the import system calls), the values it can read at its start, kept where the code that ran names them (`value_read`, `_TRIGGERS`: module-level names, a string constant counted as a name; os.environ, the working folder and the decimal context when the code names them; its class's attributes along the MRO; a loaded product module's attributes, the module held or imported, in a function too) and which of those modules are loaded, and its functions' return values and each local (digests to `LIMIT` elements and `DEPTH` levels, an object of the default repr by its attributes, a dataclass by its fields, a StringIO by its value, an int that is a held object's id() a placeholder; a value of `FINE` elements or fewer a finer record too: digit runs masked, the runs, `PIECES` pieces, a short repr); compared by `part_problems`, each part by itself (what the three BASE runs agree on equal in each HEAD run, `_sequence` for assertions and children; a name BASE read that HEAD lacks, its module loaded at the part's start, absent (`_value_at`; one of OLD's a new file lacks, where the code looked it up on the module or a BASE run's value is not what OLD's import left, `_since_import`; a constant not compared, the mover refusing `globals()` and `sys.modules[name]` reads), one whose module is not loaded there counted; product lines every BASE run reached reached in each HEAD run; a loop's count off by a few, alone, labelled: rerun it); what the BASE runs disagree on compared finer (`_compare`: a HEAD value one BASE run's dropped, some BASE run lacking it, `_matches`; the digit runs they disagree on masked, each within BASE's range widened by its spread, 2 at least (or, adjacent ones, a timestamp's fields, between BASE's read together, each later one of a fixed width and two integer digits at most, `_between`; one not numeric of their length and kinds of character, `_run_fits`) and listed beside HEAD's, one outside dropped, `_runs_level`; then piece by piece; assertions and children site by site), what is left or a cut digest refused, the BASE runs' values shown, unless HEAD's acceptance table, scripts/step9-unstable.tsv (`UNSTABLE`, `read_unstable`; listed by the text check, `unstable_text_problems`) accepts it, each acceptance listed (an accepted line count still nonzero where BASE's is and within BASE's range widened by 2 or a tenth). The table's rules: a reason written by hand, naming the source and its line (`UNSTABLE_SOURCES`, `:NNN`); a `local` or `arg` row only once the reviewer has read that nothing else in the value can depend on an earlier class; a `line` row only for a poll or deadline loop; no `assertions` or `child processes` row (refused) and no `cut` row unread: prep the module; a row nothing dropped goes; more than about five rows for a test: prep it or move it whole; every "digit runs left out" note read, each run a clock's, a pid's or a duration's; a row accepting a match (a HEAD value some BASE run lacks) saying why the value is flaky at BASE; a row for a digit run outside BASE's spread naming what the number is (an inode, a thread ident, a pid) and the line that makes it. Every test passing at BASE (`base_problems`: a pass, an expected failure, or `@unittest.skip` applied directly with a constant reason; a fixture's error or skip refused), the ids one to one (counted, whatever `--no-ids`), no test of a class no line places, no test that traces itself, the outcomes the same in every run. It proves an honest batch; it is no sandbox for one written to fool it. `scripts/moved.py` — `formerly_map`, `normalize`, `where`, `split_modules`, `test_classes`: where an old test module, class or test went (a split module: each file it went to) (a backlog or CHANGELOG reference, dotted or as a path), read from the `Formerly:` lines; exit 1 when it never moved or its class or test is not where the module went.
- `scripts/mutation_triage.py` — `main`: groups mutation survivors for review.
- `.github/workflows/tests.yml` — `check-pii.sh`, `check_tax_rules.py`, `gitleaks-regression.sh`, `pr-commits`, `ubuntu-24.04`: the same gate on pull requests and pushes to main (gitleaks and the per-commit PII scan with the base commit's config and scanner); every job that runs on a pull request runs on every one (land.sh waits for them all); the nightly `schedule` runs on the public repo only (`github.repository == 'taxjson/taxjson'`); the dev repo's jobs run on its self-hosted runner (`taxjson-dev`), each in a fresh virtualenv, and gitleaks is pinned per `RUNNER_ARCH`.
- `.github/workflows/privacy-watch.yml` — `check-public.sh --days 2`, `schedule`: the daily read-only scan of what the public repo serves beside the code (daily there only).
- `tests/features/repo_checks/test_release_and_ci_hardening.py` — `TestWorkflowPins`, `TestRunners`, `TestEveryPullRequestCheck`, `TestGitleaks`, `TestReleaseWorkflow`, `TestLand`, `TestCheckPublicDays`: action and runner pins, every pull-request check on every pull request, gitleaks, release.yml, land.sh.

## Tests

Run the suite from `tests/` with `TAXJSON_WIDTH=0`. Engine changes are pinned
by property fuzzers and by tests that run both countries side by side.
Style tests build small synthetic projects and check every line printed.

- `tests/tax_rules/dual.py` — `gains_both`, `projects_both`, `cli_both`, `settings_for`, `tx`: one book or project run under both countries.
- `tests/_golden.py` — `books`, `produce`, `compare`, `diff`, `update`, `--update`, `_WORK_COMPARED`, `_STAMPS`, `project_run` (a project book copied and run, its exit checked; `tests/test_model_evidence_books.py` reads its work/ rows): the golden ledgers, whole synthetic books under `tests/golden/`, every output but the stages' intermediate files, JSON and TOML numbers to float noise, text lines exactly (`tests/test_golden_ledgers.py`). A refactor leaves them alone. A book's `views` (`_run_views`, `view_name`, `view_width`, `view_tool`, `view_argv`, `copies.json`): `tjs` commands run after the run, each at width 0 or its own `width` (120: what a person piping it sees, named `<name>_w120`), or a taxjson-* tool's module (`tool`: a document no command prints; `view_argv` starts with its console script, `console_scripts`), their output and the files they write recorded under `expected/_views/` (`tests/test_golden_views.py`); every project book a thousand days later (`clock_days`, `tests/test_golden_clock.py`) through `tests/_clockshift/sitecustomize.py` — `TAXJSON_TEST_CLOCK_DAYS`, `_DateMeta`, `_RealClassesFor`, and on Python 3.11's float sum() through `tests/_oldsum/sitecustomize.py` — `TAXJSON_TEST_OLD_SUM`, `old_sum` (TJ-2791); these and the open tracer chain the next sitecustomize through `tests/_shimchain.py` — `load_next`.
- `tests/_style.py` — `project`, `Project`, `assert_styled`, `assert_console`, `assert_labelled`: synthetic projects and output-style asserts.
- `tests/_support/__init__.py` — `_hermetic`: what test modules share (helpers, fixture text, rows), a module per former source, each naming it on a `Shared from:` line; a test module imports from here, never from another test module (`tests/test_test_layout.py` — `CROSS_IMPORTS_MAX`); each module's name starts with a broker, country or map-section prefix (`tests/test_test_layout.py` — `SUPPORT_PREFIXES`, `unprefixed_support`). Tests two modules run, each in its own setting, are a mixin here named `<Subject>Cases`, each module's TestCase built on it (`tests/_support/ib_conid_renames.py` — `_Layout`, `NewEventBeforeNewFirstTradeCases`, `CorporateActionRenamesCases`, `DatesPerContractIdCases`, `OldRowAfterNewFirstRowCases`, `DeclarationsAndPlaceholdersCases`: the IB one-contract-id ticker-change cases, run by `tests/rules/ca/acb/test_contract_id_ticker_changes.py` in a single-folder project and by `tests/rules/ca/acb/test_contract_id_ticker_changes_in_a_year_folder.py` — `_shared_run` in year folders, each class's `_run`; `tests/test_test_layout.py` — `support_mixin_problems`, `misnamed_support_mixins`, `test_a_support_mixin_of_tests_is_named_cases`).
- `tests/_paths.py` — `REPO`, `SRC`, `TESTS`, `EXAMPLES`, `FIXTURES`: the repository's folders for a test module or a helper wherever it sits below tests/ (never a `__file__` depth path, which names the wrong folder once a module moves); `tests/test_test_layout.py` — `_own_file_uses`, `_OWN_FILE_OK` (the sitecustomize shims keep their own file), `test_every_folder_of_test_modules_is_a_package` refuses a depth path and a folder of test modules without `__init__.py` (discovery skips it silently); `load_tests_files`, `test_no_module_builds_its_own_suite` refuses a `load_tests` in a test module or helper (what a suite runs is then the runner's by-class check's to see); `ROUND_NAMED_MAX`, `_ROUND_NAMED`, `_ROUND_WORDS`, `_NOT_ROUND_NAMED`, `_round_named`, `test_the_round_named_modules_only_fall`: the round-named modules (after a fix round, a review, an audit) only fall in number (step 9 moves them); `CROSS_IMPORTS_MAX`, `cross_imports`, `_LOADERS`, `_patch_kind`, `_patch_target`, `test_test_modules_share_through_support_only_fall`: the modules (test modules and their helpers, in tests/ and packages/*/tests) that import a test module (relative imports too), name one as a patch target, or load one by name or path only fall in number (step 9 B1 moves what they share to tests/_support/); `bound_test_classes`, `test_case_classes`, `_module_scope`, `_never_imported`, `held_test_cases`, `test_no_test_class_runs_twice`, `test_no_helper_holds_a_test_case`: no module binds at its module scope (a from-import, relative, star or in an if or try, or `module.Class` assigned) a TestCase class with tests of its own (or through a base in its module) that another test module defines, and no helper holds one (unittest would run its tests in both, under one id): a quick check of the source; the runner's collect checks the classes themselves; `home_at_import`, `reaching_test_modules`, `given_home_bindings`, `test_a_module_run_alone_binds_no_given_home`: each test module whose module-scope imports reach a module that reads HOME at its import (lib/rate_cache's and lib/crypto_prices' cache paths), imported alone in a fresh interpreter with a HOME of its own, imports none of them while HOME is still that one (it imports tests/_hermetic first). `packages/taxjson-fetch/tests/_bootstrap.py` — `_setup`, `PLUGIN_SRC`, `REPO_ROOT`: imported first by every plugin test module (the paths, the entry point); not `_support`, which is the core's package of shared helpers. What the plugin's tests share is a module of their tree's own named for it, imported after `_bootstrap` (`packages/taxjson-fetch/tests/_questrade_fetch.py` — `_qt_request`, `_live`, `_parser_refuses`: the Questrade fetch's rows, request and live run).
- `tests/_engine.py` — `modules`, `files`, `binding`, `binders`, `patch_engine`, `store_engine`, `source`, `file_with`, `site_pattern`, `loader`: the gains engine's seams for the tests (step 10 of the engine refactor): each module of the engine package (src/taxjson/engine/; `scripts/move_defs.py` — `B0B_HELPER`). A test patches an engine name in every module binding it (`patch_engine`; `store_engine` and `loader` for a child's planted sitecustomize), reads the engine's text (`source`), finds a coverage marker's file (`file_with`, for `tests/_tripwire.py` — `Tripwire`) or matches a path the engine names (`site_pattern`) through it, never through one module, which would reach that module alone; `tests/test_engine_seams.py` — `TestTheModules`, `TestPatchEngine`, `TestTheFiles`: it reaches every module of the engine (and lib/core.py, the re-export shim the batches left, is gone); `tests/test_engine_layout.py` — `TestTheEngineLayers`, `JOINS`: no module of engine/ca imports engine/us or the reverse, a shared one imports neither, only `taxjson.engine.by_country` both, and none imports relatively; `TestTheCountryLeaf`, `TestTheEngineLoadsNoProject`: parsers/, characterize/ and engine/ import the country leaf, never lib/country.py, and importing the engine loads no module of taxjson.project (`src/taxjson/lib/loss_overrides.py` — `read_project` imports it when it runs).
- `tests/test_moved_tests.py` — `TestFormerlyLines`, `formerly_problems`, `TestDocstringLine`, `TestTheMover`, `TestTheVerifiersTextCheck`, `TestTheVerifiersRuns`, `_synthetic`, `TestTheSplit`, `TestTheSplitsPieces`, `TestTheVerifierOnASplit`, `TestTheReviewsCases`, `TestWhatAHelperRunsAtImport`, `TestTheOutcomes`, `REVIEW`, `REVIEW2`, `TestTheSecondReviewsCases`, `TestTheSecondReviewsOutcomes`, `REVIEW3`, `REVIEW3_SUPPORT`, `CRITIQUE`, `CLEAN`, `CLEAN_EXTRA`, `TestTheThirdReviewsCases`, `TestTheFourthRoundsMover`, `REVIEW5`, `REVIEW5_EXTRA`, `TestTheFifthRoundsCases`, `REVIEW6`, `REVIEW6_EXTRA`, `_rows6`, `TestTheSixthRoundsCases`, `SPLIT_OLD`, `_fake_ruff`: every `Formerly:` line names a module gone from the top of tests/, none moved whole twice but a split one (each of its files names it, each class there that runs tests has its line), and `scripts/moved.py` finds each; the docstring line (characters, line ends, a shebang), the whole-path rename; the mover on a synthetic repository (the module renamed in git, its docstring line, the packages, the baseline, the scripts' and the workflow's dotted names, the ratchet, history left alone, the mentions it lists), a batch it refuses before any change (untracked, ignored, a name another module has), one it undoes, a write all or nothing, its dry run, line ends through a move, a script module moved; the verifier's text check from tree to tree passing a clean move, listing docstrings, docs and settled names, failing each edit a move must not carry (a deletion, the baseline, a mode, a symlink, a hidden staged edit, a copy, a new or executable `__init__.py`, a file not text, a rename or the ratchet reverted, a move by hand the mover would refuse) and each settled name but that move's own; the --summary and wiring comparisons; the base's copy running isolated; replace refs refused; the ancestry check. A split on a synthetic repository (ruff stood in by `_fake_ruff`): the fragments' text exact (a helper, a hook, a mixin, comments, CRLF line ends, a trimmed import, made and extended class docstrings), git, the baseline, a script's `OLD.Class`, the ids one to one, the new files run; what every file holds; each refusal before any change, a new finding undone; the verifier passing it and failing it tampered (a line changed or moved, a call at import, a helper, a comment or a class dropped, a constant changed in a copy, a class copied, a class line dropped or naming another module, a hook missing, a mode, the module kept, the baseline's counts, a file left as the base's) or cut by hand where the mover refuses; the reviews' cases (state two files would share: an argument changed, an object's own method, a mixin's list, `__init_subclass__`, a decorator registry, a metaclass; a second name, a made class, a module importing itself; a decorator whose store runs with the test, module data read through read-only methods, split; an import that runs code (a store, a call, a change, through another helper or a package's `__init__`; the runner's setup apart), in every file); each outcome recorded (a skip's reason, a subtest's failure, an exception's type, a fixture's error, a run that fails, the seed out of the test's environment) with what each test and fixture does (lines, assertions and their sites, an argument each), only an unconditional `@unittest.skip` by hand (not an alias, a named reason, a condition), each part compared (a line, an assertion's argument or message, a child's line, variable, folder or exit, a value read or unset, a local, a return value, a product line fewer; what the BASE runs disagree on and a cut dropped, a difference any HEAD run shows, a loop's count labelled), the values a part reads by the names its code holds, the unstable table read and listed by the text check, BASE's failures, skips and fixture skips refused, a part dropped refused until the table accepts it, the outcomes kept, order dependence refused (the outcome runs use 3.12's `sys.monitoring`); the second reviews' cases (state each copy would hold: a class attribute aliased or passed to a call, a module list passed, aliased or handed out, a mutable default, a cache, `__subclasses__`, `__mro__`, `__file__`; a class a call makes; a copy only read, split; every refusal at once; an unread decorated def in every file; a run file's comment listed, the gate test's stems; process state and a copy's state seen by the test that reads it, state no test reads refusing nothing; an id counted twice; a class no line places); the third review's cases (each refused by the mover, a `global` name, a temp folder or an open file copied, or failed by the verifier, named) and the critique's (a helper's list or a copied one checked through C, a decimal context leaked, a child reading a file under HOME, a product branch an environment variable opened), clean splits passing (helpers copied, module fixtures, temp folders seeded, a value naming OLD, a map keyed by id(), in a dataclass too, a product module one new file does not import (counted); a poll whose count each run's turn sets refused, its counts shown, then accepted by its keys; a clock value compared with its digits masked, never a difference); the finer comparison (digit runs, pieces, a site's assertions, a name absent where its module is loaded, an accepted count's bounds); the fifth round's cases (a fixture that skips, a fixture's snapshot of the environment, the decimal context read through Decimal or beside a clock, an assertion's message holding the clock, a poll, a child printing the clock or what it inherited or exiting on it, randomness an earlier class seeded, the environment read in one expression, a class attribute or a helper's list read by a string, a product function's cache, a StringIO's value, a class body run in a test, a value nested past the digest's depth, a StringIO two files would hold), each refused or failed; the sixth round's (a helper's name made at its first note, a timestamp beside an hour in a dict or printed, a poll that stops running, a skip a condition binds, a loop three against two), each refused or failed; the seventh round's (OLD's list read through `sys.modules` by a name or a helper, a stamp an earlier class's time zone shifts, a leak only one BASE run shows), each refused or failed; the eighth round's (a clock an hour off, a timestamp's later field past a clock's width, a value most BASE runs hold), each failing without a row and passing with one, the real wraps left out, the lookup probe in neither `dir()` nor `vars()`; the timing cases settled (a run's turn from a numbered file each run makes, `zzturn`, not a poll; a stamp to the microsecond; a marker made exclusive), so a loaded machine changes no pinned reason; a constant a new file lacks not compared, every note in full, a product module imported in a function counted, a reason that names no source and line refused; a comment on a docstring's closing line refused, the branches on the machine listed, the verifier refusing a Python before 3.12).
- `tests/_tmpfiles.py` — `private_tmpfile`, `private_dir`: temp files each alone in a private folder, so a parser's sibling discovery never reads another test's or run's file.
- `tests/_planted_rates.py` — `plant`, `mirror`, `merge_into`, `cleanup`: a test's hand-written work/to_base.csv, its rates also in the HOME's currency cache, so a run that rebuilds the file (a source file newer than it, or rates that end in the past) writes the same rates back; the suite's shared synthetic HOME is put back by the test's cleanup.
- `tests/test_temp_isolation.py` — `TestNoSharedTempRoot`, `TestStrayLedgersInTheTempRoot`: no test writes into the shared temp root; stray Kraken ledgers in TMPDIR break nothing.
- `tests/test_engine_invariants.py` — `TestConservationFuzz`, `TestOrderInvariance`, `TestCraGoldenExamples`: engine fuzzing and golden examples; the random books and the conservation check in `tests/_support/engine_fuzz_books.py` — `make_book`, `_check_conservation`.
- `tests/test_conservation.py` — `TestShareCountChecker`, `TestStrandedBasisChecker`: share and cost conservation checks.
- `tests/test_transfer_fuzz.py` — `TestTransferFuzz`: transfer fuzzing; its books in `tests/_support/engine_transfer_fuzz_books.py` — `make_transfer_book`.
- `tests/test_settle_straddle_fuzz.py` — `TestSettleStraddleFuzz`, `conservation_gap`: settlement-straddle fuzzing; its books in `tests/_support/engine_settle_fuzz_books.py` — `build_book`.
- `tests/test_partition_foundation.py` — `TestOneCountryResolver`, `TestSettingOwnership`, `TestCommandOwnership`: the country partition.
- `tests/test_output_style.py` — `TestWidth`, `TestWrapAndMessages`: the output style.
- `tests/test_dependency_locks.py` — `TestLocksMatchPyproject`, `TestCheckCatchesDrift`, `TestInstallerUsesTheLocks`: the locks match pyproject.toml's extras and taxjson-fetch's dependencies, are not hand-edited, and the installer installs only from them.
- `tests/test_check_pii.py` — `TestTreeScan`, `TestDiffAndPush`: the PII scanner.
- `tests/test_knowledge_pack.py` — `TestArchitectureMap`, `TestReferences`: every path and symbol this map names still exists.
- `tests/test_fast_gate.py` — `TestPlan`, `TestRunner`, `TestGateRecord`, `TestReleaseReusesAPass`: the parallel runner, the PASS records and their reuse by release.sh.
- `run_tests.sh` — `unittest`: runs the suite with the project venv.
